## Q80. If every ordinal-length Lipschitz game G_(δ)(U,W) between countably complete ult…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.5.62) · **Collection** 1

If every ordinal-length Lipschitz game G_(δ)(U,W) between countably complete ultrafilters is determined, must UA hold?

**Context.** Origin: Goldberg proposes these questions in his analysis of ultrafilter comparison and the Ultrapower Axiom (UA); some also circulated in his research papers.

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. Advisor(s): W. Hugh Woodin. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://www.math.harvard.edu/gabriel-goldberg-awarded-sacks-prize/) Location: Question 3.5.62, printed p.85 (PDF p.92), §3.5; games defined in §3.5. Follow-on evidence: Kaplan's 2026 paper explicitly retains the equivalent linearity question as open (Q5.2). Its counterexamples separating the two orders do not settle this question.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.12835v1#S5)

*Duplicate of Qc5679e78d00747f41b60.*

## Q85. For n nonconstant solution pairs of ∂\_(x)^(2)y=6y^(2)+x with (y_(i)/y_(j))^(5)≠1…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.4.1) · **Collection** 1

For n nonconstant solution pairs of ∂\_(x)^(2)y=6y^(2)+x with (y_(i)/y_(j))^(5)≠1 for i≠j, must trdeg_(C) C(bar(x),bar(y),∂\_(bar(x))bar(y))≥2n+1? Here ∂\_(x)=D/(Dx).

**Context.** Origin: These are Aslanyan’s inequality and classification questions, also developed in his own preprints; the thesis supplies partial results.

**Source.** Vahagn Aslanyan. *Ax-Schanuel Type Inequalities in Differentially Closed Fields*. University of Oxford, 2017. Advisor(s): Boris Zilber; Jonathan Pila. [primary source](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3Abced8c2d-22df-4a21-9a1f-5e4204b6c85d/files/m0f067c1ec87aff1a60282e5d23c671d4) · [record](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3Abced8c2d-22df-4a21-9a1f-5e4204b6c85d) Location: Conjecture 7.4.1, §7.4, printed p.153 in repository version (corpus PDF p.160); equation (4.7). Author-hosted differently paginated version: same Conjecture 7.4.1. Follow-on evidence: Restated as Conj.3.15 in the 2021 journal paper. Checked current author bibliography and targeted searches; no proof or counterexample located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apal.2020.102871) · [2](https://vahagn-aslanyan.github.io/research.html)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3.2) · **Collection** 1

For divergent AD^(+) models L(A,ℝ),L(B,ℝ), set Δ=𝒫(ℝ)∩L(A,ℝ)∩L(B,ℝ) and κ=min(Θ^(L(A,ℝ)),Θ^(L(B,ℝ))). Must L_(κ)(⋃\_(α<κ)α^(ω),Δ) satisfy ZF? Divergence means same reals and mutually noncontained collections of sets of reals.

**Source.** Douglas W Blue. *Models of the axiom of determinacy and the foundations of set theory*. Harvard University, 2023. Advisor(s): W. Hugh Woodin. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/514d144e-83cd-4616-9de3-5d0346f7bbb8/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/entities/publication/882221ab-9479-43eb-b90c-24db981353bc) Location: Question 1.3.2, §1.3, printed p. 45 (PDF p. 54); divergent models and Δ(A,B) defined on printed pp. 44–45. Search-qualified status: Open in the thesis; no subsequent resolution of this exact common-part construction was found. Berkeley’s July 2025 problem list still asks broader divergent-model strength questions, but that does not independently certify this exact question.

**Further links.** [1](https://doug.blue/) · [2](https://math.berkeley.edu/~goldberg/conference/ProblemListBerkeley25.pdf) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.18958v1)

## Q113. If an abstract linear space has irreducible algebraic base S and constant fibre …

If an abstract linear space has irreducible algebraic base S and constant fibre dimension n, is every interpretation in the compact-complex-space structure A definably isomorphic over S(A) to a rank-n holomorphic linear space?

**Context.** Origin: Author's proposed upgrade of his Theorem 4.2.4 from piecewise holomorphic local trivialisations to a global holomorphic linear-space interpretation.

Advisor identified in acknowledgments. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: No exact resolution located. Moosa–Pillay (December 2025), Q3.3, still ask a closely related definable-vector-space realization question; this supports ongoing difficulty, not equivalence of the two formulations.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~rmoosa/nontrivialH1-revised.pdf#page=15) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19524)

## Q146. For singular α>κ and κ α-subcompact, can preparatory forcing make κ's α-subcompa…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.3.6) · **Collection** 2

For singular α>κ and κ α-subcompact, can preparatory forcing make κ's α-subcompactness indestructible under every <κ-directed closed forcing in Hα, extending Theorem 3.3.1 beyond regular α?

**Context.** Secondary advisor: Dugald Macpherson. Origin: The forcing questions extend the author’s work. Membership-reduct problems are joint with Howe and Mennuni, preprinted in 2019. Additional definitions: For the membership-reduct questions, ZFA means ZFC with Foundation replaced by Anti-Foundation, in ambient ZFC+Con(ZFC). Put S(x,y)⇔(x∈y or y∈x) and D(x,y)⇔(x∈y and y∈x).

**Source.** Beatrice Adam-Day. *Indestructibility and C^(n)-Supercompact Cardinals*. University of Leeds, 2023. Advisor(s): Andrew Brooke-Taylor. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/34601/1/Bea%20A-D%20Thesis.pdf) · [record](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/34601/) Location: Question 3.3.6, printed p.47 (PDF p.56); preceding Theorem 3.3.1, printed p.40. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: No later singular-level solution found. Checked the primary thesis, later supervisor conference report, and targeted indestructibility literature searches.

**Further links.** [1](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/34601/1/Bea%20A-D%20Thesis.pdf#page=56) · [2](https://lc2024.github.io/speakers/andrew-brooke-taylor/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.7.1) · **Collection** 2

Is the following problem NP-complete? Given an explicit finite set L of ground terms and k≥0, decide whether a totally rigid acyclic tree grammar G with at most k productions satisfies L⊆L(G). The number of nonterminals is unrestricted. Extra generated terms are allowed; size counts productions, not total symbols.

**Context.** Origin: Joint Eberhard–Ebner–Hetzl question, published before dissertation filing. Status evidence for this section, checked 5 October 2026: Retained from the 2018 joint paper as open in the 2021 thesis. Exact-name searches and the advisor’s publication list updated March 2026 revealed no resolution. Status source 1: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-98654-8_24
Status source 2: https://gebner.org/pdfs/2018-09-09_tratcompl.pdf
Status source 3: https://www.dmg.tuwien.ac.at/hetzl/research/index.html Setup for question 160: Tree productions have a single nonterminal on the left. Acyclicity forbids cycles in nonterminal dependencies; total rigidity requires every occurrence of a nonterminal to use the same production during a derivation.

**Source.** Gabriel Ebner. *Inductive theorem proving based on tree grammars*. TU Wien, 2021. Advisor(s): Stefan Hetzl. [primary source](https://repositum.tuwien.at/bitstream/20.500.12708/16832/1/Ebner%20Gabriel%20-%202021%20-%20Inductive%20theorem%20proving%20based%20on%20tree%20grammars.pdf) · [record](https://repositum.tuwien.at/handle/20.500.12708/16832?mode=full) Location: Open Problem 3.7.1, p.72 (PDF p.72); Problem 3.7.2, p.71; Definitions 2.5.2–2.6.2, pp.33–34. Status: see the section-wide dated evidence above.

<a id="q240"></a>

## Q240. Is D uniformly definable in A, or in B?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Is D uniformly definable in A, or in B?

**Context.** Metadata note: Research supervisors listed above. Formal promotores: Robert van Rooij and Maria Aloni; copromotor: Fan Yang. Origin: Joint definability questions with Søren Brinck Knudstorp; collaboration credited in the thesis and March 2025 preprint. Shared setup for questions 240, 241, 242: Teams are subsets \$t\subseteq2^X\$, with fixed finite \$X\$. Atoms hold at every valuation; conjunction requires both conjuncts; \$\bot\$ holds only at \$\varnothing\$. Define \$t\models\varphi\to\psi\$ iff every \$s\subseteq t\$ supporting \$\varphi\$ supports \$\psi\$, and \$t\models\Diamond\varphi\$ iff some nonempty \$s\subseteq t\$ supports \$\varphi\$. Let \$D(\varphi,\psi)\$ mean \$\exists s,u\\,[t\subseteq s\cup u,\ s\models\varphi,\ u\models\psi]\$, and \$G(\varphi,\psi)\$ mean \$\exists s\supseteq t\\,[s\models\varphi\text{ or }s\models\psi]\$. Language A uses atoms, \$\bot,\land,\to,\Diamond\$; B adds G; C uses atoms, \$\bot,\land,D,\Diamond\$, functional-dependence atoms, and negated classical formulas. A dependence atom requires valuations agreeing on its input variables to agree on its output variable. Classical formulas use atoms, \$\bot,\land,D\$ and classical negation only. Uniform definability requires one formula context for every eligible argument pair.

**Source.** Aleksi Ilari Anttila. *Not Nothing: Nonemptiness in Team Semantics*. University of Amsterdam, 2025. Advisor(s): Maria Aloni; Fan Yang. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2350/1/DS-2025-04.text.pdf) · [record](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2350/) Location: §3.4, Open problem(s) 1, printed p.87 (PDF p.107).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21850) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08122) · [3](https://aianttila.github.io/) · [4](https://knudstorp.github.io/research/)

Atoms hold at every valuation; conjunction requires both conjuncts; \$\bot\$ holds only at \$\varnothing\$. Define \$t\models\varphi\to\psi\$ iff every \$s\subseteq t\$ supporting \$\varphi\$ supports \$\psi\$, and \$t\models\Diamond\varphi\$ iff some nonempty \$s\subseteq t\$ supports \$\varphi\$. Let \$D(\varphi,\psi)\$ mean \$\exists s,u\\,[t\subseteq s\cup u,\ s\models\varphi,\ u\models\psi]\$, and \$G(\varphi,\psi)\$ mean \$\exists s\supseteq t\\,[s\models\varphi\text{ or }s\models\psi]\$. Language A uses atoms, \$\bot,\land,\to,\Diamond\$; B adds G; C uses atoms, \$\bot,\land,D,\Diamond\$, functional-dependence atoms, and negated classical formulas. A dependence atom requires valuations agreeing on its input variables to agree on its output variable. Classical formulas use atoms, \$\bot,\land,D\$ and classical negation only. Uniform definability requires one formula context for every eligible argument pair.

**Source.** Aleksi Ilari Anttila. *Not Nothing: Nonemptiness in Team Semantics*. University of Amsterdam, 2025. Advisor(s): Maria Aloni; Fan Yang. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2350/1/DS-2025-04.text.pdf) · [record](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2350/) Location: §3.4, Open problem(s) 3, printed p.88 (PDF p.108); generalized uniform definability, Definition 3.4.3.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21850) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08122) · [3](https://aianttila.github.io/) · [4](https://knudstorp.github.io/research/)

## Q243. Do IVL and IVLM have the finite model property: does each non-theorem admit a co…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Do IVL and IVLM have the finite model property: does each non-theorem admit a countermodel on a finite birelational frame validating the relevant logic? Negation is intuitionistic; the modalities are not Boolean duals.

**Context.** Origin: Residual questions about the author’s vagueness logics, developed in his 2022 AiML paper. Shared setup for questions 243, 244: Let S comprise frames (W,≤,R), with ≤ a partial order and R reflexive/transitive, satisfying R;≤⊆≤;R and R⁻¹;≤⊆≤;R⁻¹ (semicolon reads left-to-right). Valuations are upward closed; □φ at w requires φ at every v with w≤uRv, and ◇φ requires some wRv satisfying φ. FSS4 is the corresponding Fischer Servi logic. IVL=FSS4+(¬¬◇p→◇p); IVLM=IVL+M, where M=¬□(◇p∧¬□p).

**Source.** Ahmee Christensen. *Semantic Studies of Modal and Intuitionistic Modal Logics*. University of California, Berkeley, 2025. Advisor(s): Wesley H. Holliday. ## Q245. Determine the computational complexity of satisfiability for the full, arbitrari…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.6.15) · **Collection** 3

Determine the computational complexity of satisfiability for the full, arbitrarily nested language generated by atoms, Boolean connectives, ≿, ≻, ♦ and ⟨φ⟩ψ. In particular, is it NP-complete?

**Context.** Origin: Joint with Wesley Holliday and Thomas Icard; previously Problem 6.15 of their 2021 imprecise-probability article. Setup for question 245: A model consists of nonempty W, a valuation V into a field F of subsets of W, and a nonempty set P of finitely additive probability measures on F. Write [[φ]]P for its truth set. φ≿ψ (respectively φ≻ψ) requires µ([[φ]]P)≥µ([[ψ]]P) (respectively >) for every µ∈P. ♦φ requires µ([[φ]]{µ})>0 for some µ∈P. Put Pφ={µ(·|[[φ]]{µ}):µ∈P, µ([[φ]]{µ})>0}. Then ⟨φ⟩ψ holds at w exactly when Pφ is nonempty and ψ holds at w in (W,V,Pφ).

**Source.** Yifeng Ding. *Propositional Quantification and Comparison in Modal Logic*. University of California, Berkeley, 2021. Advisor(s): Wesley H. Holliday. ## Q248. Does every formula admitting a k-strong honest definition admit one of degree 1?…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.4.11) · **Collection** 3

Does every formula admitting a k-strong honest definition admit one of degree 1? The defining formulas and N depend only on φ; parameter tuples may repeat elements. Degree one may use different formulas and longer parameter tuples.

**Context.** Origin: Original research questions; the higher-arity honest-definition questions also appear in Tong’s July 2026 preprint. Shared setup for questions 248, 249: Fix a complete theory T and k≥2. For φ(x₁,…,xₖ;y), write x=(x₁,…,xₖ). A degree-N k-strong honest definition consists of fixed formulas ψᵢ(x_{≠i},y,zᵢ) (i≤k) and ψₖ₊₁(x,zₖ₊₁): for every M⊨T, finite B⊆M with |B|≥2, and a∈M^{|x|}, choose cᵢʲ∈B^{|zᵢ|} (j≤N) so that each b∈B^{|y|} has j with a⊨Cⱼ(x,b)⊢[φ(x;b)↔φ(a;b)], where Cⱼ=ψₖ₊₁(x,cₖ₊₁ʲ)∧⋀ᵢψᵢ(x_{≠i},b,cᵢʲ).

**Source.** Ho Wang Mervyn Tong. *Distality to and from Combinatorics*. University of Leeds, 2025. Advisor(s): Pantelis Eleftheriou; Vincenzo Mantova. ## Q249. If T is strongly k-distal, without assuming NIP, must every formula φ(x₁,…,xₖ;y)…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.4.15) · **Collection** 3

If T is strongly k-distal, without assuming NIP, must every formula φ(x₁,…,xₖ;y) admit a k-strong honest definition? Strong k-distality means: for dense endpoint-free infinite tuple sequences I₀,I₁ and parameter sets B₁,…,Bₖ, if I₀+I₁ is indiscernible over their union and inserting a preserves indiscernibility over every union omitting one Bᵢ, then insertion preserves indiscernibility over the full union.

**Context.** Origin: Original research questions; the higher-arity honest-definition questions also appear in Tong’s July 2026 preprint. Shared setup for questions 248, 249: Fix a complete theory T and k≥2. For φ(x₁,…,xₖ;y), write x=(x₁,…,xₖ). A degree-N k-strong honest definition consists of fixed formulas ψᵢ(x_{≠i},y,zᵢ) (i≤k) and ψₖ₊₁(x,zₖ₊₁): for every M⊨T, finite B⊆M with |B|≥2, and a∈M^{|x|}, choose cᵢʲ∈B^{|zᵢ|} (j≤N) so that each b∈B^{|y|} has j with a⊨Cⱼ(x,b)⊢[φ(x;b)↔φ(a;b)], where Cⱼ=ψₖ₊₁(x,cₖ₊₁ʲ)∧⋀ᵢψᵢ(x_{≠i},b,cᵢʲ).

**Source.** Ho Wang Mervyn Tong. *Distality to and from Combinatorics*. University of Leeds, 2025. Advisor(s): Pantelis Eleftheriou; Vincenzo Mantova. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/37811/1/Tong_HWM_Mathematics_PhD_2025.pdf) · [record](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/37811/) Location: Problem 5.4.15, printed p.150 (PDF p.162); Theorem 5.4.12, printed p.147.

## Q250. Does a complete NIP theory T exist with 2&lt;DR(T)<ω?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.2) · **Collection** 3

Does a complete NIP theory T exist with 2&lt;DR(T)<ω?

**Context.** Origin: Walker’s NIP extension of an older stable-theory question, formulated in his pre-filing Distality Rank paper. Setup for question 250: DR(T) is the least positive integer m for which T is m-distal, or ω if none exists. Here m-distality requires: whenever an indiscernible tuple sequence is split at m+1 Dedekind cuts and a tuple is designated for each cut, simultaneous insertion preserves indiscernibility if insertion of every m-element subcollection does. NIP means that every formula has finite VC-dimension.

**Source.** Roland Walker. *Distality Rank and Tree Dimension*. University of Illinois at Chicago, 2023. Advisor(s): David E. Marker. [primary source](https://indigo.uic.edu/ndownloader/files/41525613) · [record](https://indigo.uic.edu/articles/thesis/Distality_Rank_and_Tree_Dimension/23661723) Location: Question 5.2, printed p.54 (PDF p.67); discussed again in Chapter 10, printed pp.87–88 (PDF pp.100–101). Definitions 3.13–14, printed p.39 (PDF p.52).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.22314v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04714) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2022.61)

## Q251. For a finite parameter-free family Φ(x;y) in a fixed first-order structure M, su…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.2.12) · **Collection** 3

For a finite parameter-free family Φ(x;y) in a fixed first-order structure M, suppose its distal density t is finite. Must some distal cell decomposition T of Φ satisfy |T(B)|=O(|B|^t) for every finite nonempty B⊆M^{|y|}, with a constant independent of B?

**Context.** Origin: Question about the author’s distal-density invariant; previously Problem 2.12 of his 2023 article. Setup for question 251: A distal cell decomposition uses fixed finite formula families: potential cells are ψ(M^{|x|};b₁,…,bₖ), bᵢ∈B; a fixed formula θψ defines an exclusion set for each potential cell, and the selected cells are exactly those for which B avoids θψ. They cover M^{|x|} and each φ(x;b), φ∈Φ and b∈B, is constant on every selected cell. Distal density is the infimum of exponents r admitting |T(B)|=O(|B|^r).

**Source.** Aaron William Anderson. *Distality in Combinatorics and Continuous Logic*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2024. Advisor(s): Artem Chernikov. [primary source](https://awainverse.github.io/research/thesis/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4qg9h3zx) Location: Problem 2.2.12 and Definitions 2.2.8–11, printed pp.32–33 (PDF pp.48–49).

## Q301. Find sound and complete axiomatizations of the full, arbitrarily nested \$K\$-only…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.1) · **Collection** 4

Find sound and complete axiomatizations of the full, arbitrarily nested \$K\$-only language for H-, N-, and S-semantics. A model has nonempty \$W\$, serial \$D\$, and a well-founded preorder \$\le_w\$ on \$W_w\subseteq W\$ with \$w\le_w v\$ for every \$v\in W_w\$. Let \$B\varphi\$ mean truth at all \$D\$-successors, and \$A\Rightarrow_w C\$ mean truth of \$C\$ at all \$\le_w\$-minimal \$A\$-worlds in \$W_w\$. Then \$K_H\varphi:=B\varphi\land(\neg\varphi\Rightarrow_w\neg B\varphi)\$; \$K_N\varphi:=B\varphi\land(\neg\varphi\Rightarrow_w\neg B\varphi)\land(\varphi\Rightarrow_w B\varphi)\$; and \$K_S\varphi:=B\varphi\land(B\varphi\Rightarrow_w\varphi)\$. Here \$B\$ and \$\Rightarrow\$ are semantic notation, not target-language connectives.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Yifeng Ding → Wesley H. Holliday: https://escholarship.org/uc/item/78w732g8
Wesley H. Holliday → Johan van Benthem and Krista Lawlor: https://philosophy.stanford.edu/people/wes-holliday Origin: Holliday introduces these formal models and states Problem 2.1 as a residual question after proving completeness for the non-nested fragment. The K-only cases are retained together as one problem; Remark 2.10’s solved richer-language S case is excluded.

**Source.** Wesley Halcrow Holliday. *Knowing What Follows: Epistemic Closure and Epistemic Logic*. Stanford University, 2012. Advisor(s): Johan van Benthem; Krista Lawlor. [primary source](https://philosophy.berkeley.edu/file/896/Holliday_Dissertation.pdf) · [record](https://philosophy.stanford.edu/people/wes-holliday) Location: Problem 2.1, printed pp. 79–80 (PDF pp. 96–97); model and semantics Definitions 2.2, 2.3, 2.6–2.7, pp. 19–21,28–30. Author-hosted revised ILLC edition.

## Q305. Let \$R\$ be real closed, \$K=R(i)\$, \$G=\mathbb G_a\times\mathbb G_m\$, and \$n>1\$. L…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Let \$R\$ be real closed, \$K=R(i)\$, \$G=\mathbb G_a\times\mathbb G_m\$, and \$n>1\$. Let \$V\subseteq G^n\$ be absolutely irreducible and perfectly rotund: \$\dim V=n\$, every nonzero integer linear combination of additive coordinates and monomial in multiplicative coordinates is nonconstant, and \$\dim(A\cdot V)>\operatorname{rank}A\$ when \$0<\operatorname{rank}A&lt;n\$. Define \$r(z,w)=((\operatorname{Re}z,|w|),(i\operatorname{Im}z,w/|w|))\$ coordinatewise, grouping the real and imaginary blocks, and let \$\check V=\overline{r(V)}^{\rm Zar}\$. If \$M=(N\mid Q)\$ with \$N,Q\in\operatorname{Mat}\_n(\mathbb Z)\$ invertible over \$\mathbb Q\$ satisfies \$\dim(M\cdot\check V)=n\$, is \$v\mapsto M\cdot r(v)\$ surjective from \$V(K)\$ onto \$(M\cdot\check V)(K)\$? Matrices act linearly on additive coordinates and by monomials on multiplicative coordinates.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Ho Wang Mervyn Tong → Vincenzo Mantova: https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/37811/

The question is part of his own involution-construction research, also presented in a pre-filing 2011 preprint.

**Source.** Vincenzo Mantova. *Zilber fields and complex exponentiation*. Scuola Normale Superiore di Pisa, 2013. Advisor(s): Alessandro Berarducci. ## Q306. In an NTP\$\_2\$ theory, let \$(a_i)\_{i\in\mathbb Z}\$ be indiscernible and \$(b_j)\_{j…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.5.15) · **Collection** 4

In an NTP\$\_2\$ theory, let \$(a_i)\_{i\in\mathbb Z}\$ be indiscernible and \$(b_j)\_{j<\omega}\$ indiscernible over \$\\{a_i:i\ne0\\}\$, with \$b_0=a_0\$. For every \$\varphi(x;y)\$, does consistency of \$\\{\varphi(x;a_i):i\in\mathbb Z\\}\$ imply consistency of \$\\{\varphi(x;b_j):j<\omega\\}\$?

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Aaron William Anderson → Artem Chernikov: https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4qg9h3zx
Artem Chernikov → Itaï Ben Yaacov: https://chernikov.umd.edu/papers.html Origin: Original questions from Chernikov’s doctoral research, including joint work incorporated into Chapters 2–3 and preprints circulated in 2012 before later journal publication. Shared setup for questions 306, 307, 308: Work in a monster model of a complete first-order theory \$T\$. \$T\$ is NTP\$\_2\$ if no \$\varphi(x;y)\$ admits \$(b_{ij})\_{i,j<\omega}\$ with each row \$\\{\varphi(x;b_{ij}):j<\omega\\}\$ \$2\$-inconsistent and every transversal \$\\{\varphi(x;b_{i,f(i)}):i<\omega\\}\$, \$f:\omega\to\omega\$, consistent.

**Source.** Artem Chernikov. *On theories without the tree property of the second kind*. Université Claude Bernard Lyon 1, 2012. Advisor(s): Itaï Ben Yaacov. ## Q309. Give a concrete sound and strongly complete natural-deduction calculus for \$\mat…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Give a concrete sound and strongly complete natural-deduction calculus for \$\mathrm{PInd}\$ in its own language \$p,\neg p,\bar u\perp\bar v,\wedge,\otimes\$, without a nonemptiness atom. Teams are sets of Boolean valuations; literals hold pointwise and \$\wedge\$ requires both conjuncts. \$X\models\varphi\otimes\psi\$ iff \$X=Y\cup Z\$, \$Y\models\varphi\$ and \$Z\models\psi\$ (overlap permitted). \$X\models\bar u\perp\bar v\$ iff \$\forall s,t\in X\ \exists w\in X\$ with \$w(\bar u)=s(\bar u)\$ and \$w(\bar v)=t(\bar v)\$. Require \$\Gamma\vdash\varphi\iff\Gamma\models\varphi\$.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Aleksi Ilari Anttila → Fan Yang: https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2350/1/DS-2025-04.text.pdf

**Source.** Fan Yang. *On Extensions and Variants of Dependence Logic: A study of intuitionistic connectives in the team semantics setting*. University of Helsinki, 2014. Advisor(s): Jouko Väänänen. [primary source](https://helda.helsinki.fi/server/api/core/bitstreams/e3d2e535-dff8-4c23-95e1-20e573a8fc3d/content) · [record](https://helda.helsinki.fi/handle/10138/43011) Location: Section 4.9, open problem 3, printed p. 125 (PDF 132); syntax/semantics Definitions 4.1.1–3, pp. 61–63 (PDF 68–70).

## Q310. Are subsumption and matching in \$\mathcal{FL}\_0\$ with input forward TBoxes \$\mat…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Are subsumption and matching in \$\mathcal{FL}\_0\$ with input forward TBoxes \$\mathrm{PSPACE}\$-complete? A forward TBox contains axioms \$\forall v_1.A_1\sqcap\cdots\sqcap\forall v_s.A_s\sqsubseteq\forall v.A\$ with \$|v_i|\leq|v|\$. Subsumption asks \$C\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal T}D\$; matching asks whether a substitution of finite \$\mathcal{FL}\_0\$ concepts for variables in \$P\$, fixing concept and role names, makes \$\sigma(P)\equiv_{\mathcal T}C\$.

**Context.** Additional supervision: Sebastian Rudolph (co-supervision stated on the author’s TU Dresden page). Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Bartosz Bednarczyk → Sebastian Rudolph: https://iccl.inf.tu-dresden.de/w/images/e/e5/BBE-thesis-final.pdf
Pavlos Marantidis → Sebastian Rudolph (co-supervision): https://lat.inf.tu-dresden.de/~pavlosfm/ Origin: Joint conjecture with Baader and Fernández Gil, published in 2018 before filing. Author’s residual non-strict-threshold variant of his joint approximate-unification work. Setup for question 310: \$\mathcal{FL}\_0\$ concepts use \$\top\$, concept names, \$\sqcap\$ and \$\forall r.C\$, with ordinary description-logic semantics; a TBox is a finite set of concept inclusions. Role words denote iterated universal restrictions.

**Source.** Pavlos Marantidis. *Quantitative Variants of Language Equations and their Applications to Description Logics*. Technische Universität Dresden, 2019. Advisor(s): Franz Baader. [primary source](https://lat.inf.tu-dresden.de/research/theses/2019/Marantidis-Diss-2019.pdf) · [record](https://tud.qucosa.de/id/qucosa%3A35639) Location: Section 8.5, printed p. 140 (PDF p. 148); Definition 8.22, p. 131; upper bounds Theorems 8.31 and 8.35.

For finite nonempty \$\Sigma\$, let \$\varphi,\psi\$ be language expressions built from finite language constants, variables, Boolean operations, and concatenation of a constant language from the left. ## Q312. Does every closed well-typed program in System \$F^{=i}\_{\omega}\$ terminate under…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Does every closed well-typed program in System \$F^{=i}\_{\omega}\$ terminate under its fine-grained call-by-value operational semantics? This is the thesis’s higher-kinded, impredicatively polymorphic calculus with internalized type equalities and discriminability, with recursive types and \$\mathsf{roll}/\mathsf{unroll}\$ removed and constructor-injectivity restricted to sums, products and arrows; \$\forall\$ and \$\exists\$ remain but are not injective.

## Q314. Is \$\operatorname{Th}(\mathbb R,+,\cdot)\$ semi-equational?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.3.3) · **Collection** 4

Is \$\operatorname{Th}(\mathbb R,+,\cdot)\$ semi-equational?

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Aaron William Anderson → Artem Chernikov: https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4qg9h3zx
Alex Jacob Mennen → Artem Chernikov: https://escholarship.org/content/qt94x8s283/qt94x8s283.pdf Origin: Joint questions with Chernikov, also formulated in the 2022 preprint underlying Chapter 3. Shared setup for questions 314, 315: A partitioned formula \$\varphi(x;y)\$ is a semi-equation if no model contains \$(a_i,b_i)\_{i\in\mathbb N}\$ with \$\varphi(a_i;b_j)\iff i\neq j\$. A complete theory is semi-equational if every partitioned formula is equivalent to a finite Boolean combination of semi-equations with the same variable partition, without adding parameters.

**Source.** Alex Jacob Mennen. *Convexity in Valued Fields and Semi-equations*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2022. Advisor(s): Artem Chernikov. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt94x8s283/qt94x8s283.pdf) · [record](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/94x8s283) Location: Problem 3.3.3, printed p. 75 (PDF 84). <a id="q527"></a>

## Q527. Counting transitive possible-world relations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

Can FOMC(∀x,y,z (E(x,y)∧E(y,z)⇒E(x,z)),n) be computed in time polynomial in n? More generally, determine its exact unary-input counting complexity.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit open-problem table. Shared setup for questions 527, 528, 529: For a fixed relational sentence Φ, FOMC(Φ,n) counts labelled structures on [n] satisfying Φ; n is the sole input, written in unary. Predicates denote arbitrary binary relations, including loops, and quantified variables may coincide unless inequalities are imposed. #P_1 is #P on unary inputs. This is not model counting for an arbitrary input graph.

**Source.** Paul Beame; Guy Van den Broeck; Eric Gribkoff; Dan Suciu. *Symmetric Weighted First-Order Model Counting*. 2015. [primary source](https://starai.cs.ucla.edu/papers/BeamePODS15.pdf) Location: §5 Table 2, Transitivity.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 527, 528, 529, checked 5 October 2026: Transitivity is reaffirmed open by Baader–Claußnitzer 2025. No resolution located for any target through 2026; two-variable and restricted-relation results do not settle them.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03391) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09174) · [3](https://lat.inf.tu-dresden.de/research/reports/2025/BaCl-LTCS-25-01.pdf) · [4](https://starai.cs.ucla.edu/papers/TaghipourAISTATS13.pdf)

<a id="q692"></a>

## Q692. Exact cost of quantified marginal-identity reasoning

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

Determine the combined complexity of MC(FO(≈)): sentence model checking with marginal-identity atoms. Published bounds: PSPACE-hard, in EXPTIME.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit complexity questions. Shared setup for questions 692, 693: Use a finite relational structure A and real distributions on assignments. Literals hold on the support; conjunction uses one team; disjunction permits convex-mixture splitting; ∃x extends by assignment-dependent distributions; ∀x adds an independent uniform coordinate. Sentences start from the empty-assignment point mass. Atoms x≈y equate tuple marginals, x ⟂\_z y assert conditional independence, and ∼ negates satisfaction. Combined complexity counts the finite encodings of A and the sentence, with no real-valued input team.

**Source.** Miika Hannula; Minna Hirvonen; Juha Kontinen; Yasir Mahmood; Arne Meier; Jonni Virtema. *Logics with probabilistic team semantics and the Boolean negation*. 2025. [primary source](https://helda.helsinki.fi/server/api/core/bitstreams/57c7cf0f-5037-49ed-9518-9aaa2a5f5576/content) Location: Table 1; §8 Theorem 8, pp. 21–22; §9 p. 25.

Does satisfiability remain decidable after adjoining real exp(t)=e^t to L_poly? Input is a finite Boolean combination of s≥t, where terms use P(α),+,×, exp and α is a Boolean event formula. Semantics is exact over probability spaces; no arbitrary-real oracle is supplied.

## Q989. A decidable logic for sampling-driven belief revision

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 10

For the §7 language, are validity and its complement effectively recognizable, and is validity recursively axiomatizable? Input is a finite formula over fixed finite O, containing rational linear probability inequalities, Booleans, K, Sb, B(φ|ω≤n),[o]φ,[θ]φ. Exclude the later temporal extension.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit question following Proposition 18. Shared setup for question 989: Models: nonempty M⊆Δ(O), continuous pla: closure(M)→[0, 1], positive on M, sup_M pla=1. K quantifies over M; Sb over at-least-as-plausible alternatives. B means truth above some attained plausibility threshold. Observation updates discard zero-likelihood worlds, multiply ranks by iid likelihood and renormalize; announcements restrict M.

**Source.** Alexandru Baltag; Soroush Rafiee Rad; Sonja Smets. *Tracking probabilistic truths: a logic for statistical learning*. 2021. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11229-021-03193-6) Location: §7, pp. 9078–9081; Definitions 1–2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 10

Is LKk decidable, and does it admit a sound complete calculus? It replaces LCD’s locally-continuous-dependence atoms with k_XY: continuity of Y at the current state in the X-pullback topology.

Joint-variable ranges have product-subspace topologies. Syntax: P(x̄), Booleans, D_Xφ, D_XY, K_Xφ, k_XY. D_X quantifies over the X-value fiber; D_XY makes Y constant there; K_X is pullback interior. Models may be infinite.

## Q994. Exact complexity of a unique undefeated minimal reason

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 10

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit §5 complexity gap. Shared setup for question 994: A finite attack graph F=(A, R) may have loops. S is admissible when conflict-free and every attacker of its members is attacked by S. An initial set is a nonempty inclusion-minimal admissible set. It is unchallenged when attacked by some argument but by no initial set.

## Q1181. Ordinary versus Blackwell determinacy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 12

Are AD and Bl-AD equivalent?

**Context.** Origin for question 1181: Martin’s conjecture, explicitly reaffirmed here. Setup for questions 1181, 1182: Work over ZF+AC_ω(R). AD_X asserts determinacy of every length-ω perfect-information game with moves in X. Bl-AD_X uses the source’s countably supported mixed strategies on alternating turns: for each payoff A⊆X^ω, some player has a strategy ensuring A is measurable and has probability 1 (respectively 0) against every opposing strategy. AD and Bl-AD mean X=ω; R=2^ω.

**Source.** Daisuke Ikegami; W. Hugh Woodin. *The Axiom of Real Determinacy and the Axiom of Real Blackwell Determinacy*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11034) Location: §8, Question 1; §2.3 for definitions.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1181, checked 6 October 2026: The 2026 journal paper leaves this open; its equivalence theorem concerns real moves under DC.

## Q1183. Computable bases from monotone bases

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7) · **Collection** 12

If a computable Banach space has a monotone Schauder basis, must it have some computable Schauder basis in that presentation?

**Context.** Origin for question 1183: Explicitly attributed to Bosserhof’s 2008 dissertation. Setup for question 1183: A computable real Banach space has an effective dense sequence and computable norm, addition and scalar multiplication. A Schauder basis gives each vector a unique norm-convergent series expansion; it is monotone when all initial-coordinate projections have norm at most one.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 13

## Q1273. Complexity of effective-dense reducibility between sets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 13

What is the descriptive complexity of {(Y,Z)∈(2^ω)² : Z≤\_ned Y}, and the index-set complexity of {(i,j): W_j≤\_ned W_i} for the standard c.e. sets W_i?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1272, 1273: Explicit paper questions; one-way reducibility on functions is settled, these targets are not. Setup for questions 1272, 1273: An effective dense description of f:ω→ω outputs f(n) or “unknown”, with unknowns of asymptotic density zero. g≤\_ned f means every such description of f Turing-computes one of g; ≡\_ned means mutual reducibility. Sets are characteristic functions.

**Source.** Peter M. Gerdes. *Comparing Notions of Dense Computability on ω^ω and 2^ω. 2025 preprint, version 1*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06925) Location: §7, Question 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1272, 1273, checked 6 October 2026: Version 1 remains current; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2508.06925v1#S7)

## Q1274. Strictly comparable 2-random degrees with one jump

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.4) · **Collection** 13

Do there exist 2-random reals x,z with x<\_T z but x′≡\_T z′?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1274, 1275, 1276: The two randomness questions are George Barmpalias’s; the maximal-almost-disjoint question is Andre Nies’s. Setup for questions 1274, 1275, 1276: Reals are in Cantor space with its fair-coin measure; 2-random means Martin-Löf random relative to ∅′. For the maximal-almost-disjoint question, an almost-disjoint family consists of infinite sets with pairwise finite intersections; maximality is restricted to infinite computable sets.

**Source.** George Barmpalias, Su Gao, Jialiang He, Takayuki Kihara, Andre Nies, Theodore Slaman, Chieu-Minh Tran, Daniel Turetsky, Philip Welch, Liang Yu, Hang Zhang. *Open Problems in Mathematical Logic*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26628v2) Location: §1, Problem 1.4(1).

## Q1277. Small-model categoricity in the one-measure model

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 13

Assume V=L[U], with sole measurable cardinal κ. If a complete recursively axiomatized second-order theory T has a model of second-order characterizable size λ<κ, must T be categorical?

**Context.** Origin for question 1277: Explicit paper question. Setup for question 1277: Use full second-order semantics. A cardinal λ is second-order characterizable if one second-order sentence in the empty vocabulary holds exactly in structures of cardinality λ.

**Source.** Tapio Saarinen, Jouko Antero Väänänen, William Hugh Woodin. *On the categoricity of complete second-order theories*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.32) Location: §9, Open Problem 2.

## Q1278. Realizing categoricity degrees by decidable structures

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 24) · **Collection** 13

Is every degree of categoricity also a degree of decidable categoricity?

**Context.** Origin for question 1278: Nikolay Bazhenov’s question. Setup for question 1278: A degree of categoricity is the least Turing degree computing isomorphisms between all computable presentations of some computable structure. For decidable categoricity use a decidable structure and only presentations with computable complete diagrams.

**Source.** George Barmpalias, Nikolay Bazhenov, Chi Tat Chong, Wei Dai, Su Gao, Jun Le Goh, Jialiang He, Keng Meng Selwyn Ng, Andre Nies, Theodore Slaman, Riley Thornton, Wei Wang, Jing Yu, Liang Yu. *Open Problems in Computability Theory and Descriptive Set Theory*. 2025. ## Q1279. Weihrauch degree of column-pivoted LU decomposition

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

Which bounds in AoUC^\* ≤\_W LU-Decomp_Q ≤\_W AoUC^⋄ are strict?

**Context.** Origin for question 1279: Original paper question; restated in Crook’s 2024 thesis and Crook–Pauly’s 2025 revision. Setup for question 1279: LU-Decomp_Q takes a finite real matrix A (Cauchy names) and outputs Q,L,U with AQ=LU: Q is a permutation matrix, U upper echelon, L lower echelon with diagonal 1. Row permutation is disallowed. AoUC chooses from a negatively presented closed subset of [0,1] promised to be a singleton or the whole interval; \* and ⋄ mean finite parallel and sequential use.

**Source.** Takayuki Kihara, Arno Pauly. *Dividing by Zero - How Bad Is It, Really?*. 2016. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.MFCS.2016.58) Location: §5; Crook–Pauly §6.

## Q1280. Winning the k-even game beyond three choices

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

For each integer k≥4, does Player I have a winning strategy in the k-even game?

**Context.** Origin for question 1280: Introduced in the 2023 preprint; restated as Tianyuan 2025 Problem 4. Setup for question 1280: In the k-even game, Player I chooses k previously unused-by-I natural numbers each round. Player II must choose an even number in the interval from their minimum to maximum, unused-by-II. II loses upon having no legal move; otherwise II wins.

**Source.** George Barmpalias, Xiaoyan Zhang, Bohua Zhan. *Compression of enumerations and gain*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.03030v2) Location: §5.2, after Theorem 5.3.

## Q1368. A local continuity-or-jump dichotomy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.1) · **Collection** 14

For reals x,y and uniformly Turing-invariant f:[x]\_T→[y]\_T, must f be continuous or y≥\_T x′?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1368, 1369: question 1368: thesis question; question 1369: height-three case of Sacks’s older embedding conjecture. Setup for questions 1368, 1369: [x]\_T is the Turing degree of a real x. Uniform invariance means one map on pairs of reduction indices converts every witnessed equivalence between inputs into a witnessed equivalence between outputs. Continuity uses Cantor-subspace topologies. Locally countable means each principal initial segment is countable.

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Results on Martin’s Conjecture*. 2021. Advisor(s): Theodore A. Slaman. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) Location: Question 9.1, p. 130; Definition 3.5, p. 49.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1368, 1369, checked 6 October 2026: Thesis questions; bounded searches found no resolution. Height-three obstruction in 2023 does not settle ZFC.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.01876v2)

## Q1370. Uniqueness in the weak Rudin–Keisler class

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 14

Is there an ultrafilter U≠U_M on D_T with U≤\_RK U_M≤\_RK U?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1370: see question 1368. Origin for questions 1370, 1371: Already posed in Lutz’s Berkeley thesis (2021), Questions 9.11 and 9.14; advisor Theodore A. Slaman. Setup for questions 1370, 1371: Work in ZF+AD. D_T is the set of Turing degrees; U_M consists of sets containing a cone. U≤\_RK V means U=f_\*V for some f:D_T→D_T. Weak RK equivalence means reducibility both ways; strict inequality excludes reverse reducibility.

**Source.** Patrick Lutz and Benjamin Siskind. *Part 1 of Martin’s Conjecture for order-preserving and measure-preserving functions*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19646v3) Location: §7, Question 4, p. 43; Definitions 5.10–5.12.

<a id="q1375"></a>

## Q1375. Complexity of bounded-alternation Büchi arithmetic

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 14

For each fixed k≥1, what is the exact decision complexity of BA_p sentences with k quantifier alternations?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1375, 1376: Source Problems 3,5,6; Problem 5 was previously posed by Haase–Różycki (2021). Setup for questions 1375, 1376: Fix p≥2 and Σ={0,…,p−1}. [L] is the set of natural numbers represented by words of L in base p. BA_p=FO(N,0,1,+,V_p,≤), where V_p(x,y) says x is the greatest p-power dividing y. Languages are supplied by finite automata.

**Source.** Christoph Haase and Mikhail Starchak. *Formal languages and arithmetic theories: recent results and open problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:3b864e1a-847b-4275-b173-fe33960e3571/files/scn69m6569) Location: Open Problem 3, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1375, 1376, checked 6 October 2026: Current source and author publications checked; later two-coprime-base decidability does not settle these targets.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Conservative fixed-block automata bound exp_(r+1)(poly(n)) for r prenex blocks; no elementary-time normalization of arbitrary BA_p sentences to equivalent Σ2 sentences. Scope: Exact complexity of each fixed-alternation fragment remains open; blocks vs switches convention left as in the question. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-032-01475-7_1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24410)


<a id="q1376"></a>

## Q1376. Recognize existentially Büchi-definable regular sets

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5) · **Collection** 14

Given regular L⊆Σ\*, is it decidable whether [L] is definable by an existential BA_p formula?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1375, 1376: Source Problems 3,5,6; Problem 5 was previously posed by Haase–Różycki (2021). Setup for questions 1375, 1376: Fix p≥2 and Σ={0,…,p−1}. [L] is the set of natural numbers represented by words of L in base p. BA_p=FO(N,0,1,+,V_p,≤), where V_p(x,y) says x is the greatest p-power dividing y. Languages are supplied by finite automata.

**Source.** Christoph Haase and Mikhail Starchak. *Formal languages and arithmetic theories: recent results and open problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:3b864e1a-847b-4275-b173-fe33960e3571/files/scn69m6569) Location: Open Problem 5, p. 7.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1375, 1376, checked 6 October 2026: Current source and author publications checked; later two-coprime-base decidability does not settle these targets.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Uniform affirmative semidecision; sound YES/NO/UNKNOWN triage of the automaton by SCC growth (polynomial growth: yes; intermediate exponential growth: no); an explicit full-growth regular set that is not existentially definable shows the triage is incomplete. Scope: A terminating algorithm or undecidability proof for all regular sets remains open. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-032-01475-7_1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24410)


<a id="q1377"></a>

## Q1377. Finite axiomatizability of Büchi arithmetic

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 14

Is BA_k finitely axiomatizable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1377, 1378: Questions 1(1) and 1(4) in the primary paper. Setup for questions 1377, 1378: Fix k≥2. BA_k=Th(N,0,S,+,V_k,≤), with successor S, V_k(0)=0 and V_k(x) the greatest k-power dividing x>0.

**Source.** Konstantin Kovalyov. *A natural axiomatization of Büchi Arithmetic*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28408) Location: Question 1(1), p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1377, 1378, checked 6 October 2026: May 2026 version is current on arXiv; no resolution found. WoLLIC proceedings metadata was checked separately.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: No axiomatization by universally quantified quantifier-free sentences, even infinitely many, in the language (0,S,+,V_k,≤). Scope: Finite axiomatizability with unrestricted quantifiers remains open. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-032-33486-2_6)

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 14

Does BA_k have an expansion by finitely many definable functions or predicates whose theory eliminates quantifiers?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1377, 1378: Questions 1(1) and 1(4) in the primary paper. Setup for questions 1377, 1378: Fix k≥2. BA_k=Th(N,0,S,+,V_k,≤), with successor S, V_k(0)=0 and V_k(x) the greatest k-power dividing x>0.

**Source.** Konstantin Kovalyov. *A natural axiomatization of Büchi Arithmetic*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28408) Location: Question 1(4), p. 13.

- **Partial result**: No QE expansion in which every added predicate and its complement are existentially definable and every added total function has an existentially definable graph. **Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-032-33486-2_6)


<a id="q1383"></a>

## Q1383. Separate Maltsev-closed quantifiers without a variable bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For r≥3, can one exhibit an isomorphism-closed class of finite structures of maximum relation arity r that is not definable in L(Q_r^M)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1383: Explicit target following Theorem 37 and in §6. Setup for question 1383: On finite relational structures, m(a,a,b)=m(b,a,a)=b; m is otherwise undefined. A quantifier Q_K is Maltsev-closed when B∈K implies A∈K for every same-universe A with relations contained in B∪m(B), coordinatewise. Q_r^M contains these quantifiers of maximum relation arity≤r. L=⋃\_k L^k_{∞ω}; L(Q_r^M) allows the indicated generalized quantifiers, without a fixed bound k.

**Source.** Anuj Dawar, Lauri Hella, Benedikt Pago. *Arity Hierarchies for Quantifiers Closed Under Partial Polymorphisms*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.9) Location: §5 after Theorem 37, pp.9:15–16; §6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1383, checked 6 October 2026: CSL 2026 proves the bounded-variable separation; the unrestricted-variable target remains explicit.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11326)


<a id="q1479"></a>

## Q1479. Halting strength with finitely generated complements

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 15

If G and H are also finitely generated, must every such selector compute ∅′?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1479, 1480: Questions 4.1 and 4.3, following Michael Deveau’s Waterloo PhD (2019), supervised by Barbara Csima; these exact targets are paper questions. Setup for questions 1479, 1480: Computable abelian E has computable subgroups E=A⊕G=B⊕H, with A≅B finitely generated. A selector is a total function returning an index of a computable G→H isomorphism from indices for E,A,B,G,H, the common rank of A,B, and finite generating tuples for A,B.

**Source.** Layth Al-Hellawi, Rachael Alvir, Barbara F. Csima, Xinyue Xie. *Effectiveness of Walker’s cancellation theorem*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1002/malq.202400030) Location: Question 4.1; Theorem 3.2.

Is it decidable, given finite automata for regular L₁,L₂⊆Σ\*, whether [L₁] is existentially definable in (N,0,1,+,[L₂],≤)?

## Q1482. A priori complexity-preserving basis theorem

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

Setup for questions 1482, 1483: KM is a priori Kolmogorov complexity; C is plain complexity; superscripts denote oracles. A pointed perfect tree is a perfect binary tree computed by each of its paths. Turing invariance means x≡\_T y implies f(x)≡\_T f(y).

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Questions*. 2026. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/questions.html) Location: Kolmogorov complexity and algorithmic randomness, first displayed KM question.

## Q1483. A pointed perfect constant-or-injective restriction

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

For every Borel Turing-invariant f:2^ω→2^ω, is there a pointed perfect T such that f↾[T] is constant or injective?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1482, 1483: Current author-stated research questions; not claimed as dissertation results. Setup for questions 1482, 1483: KM is a priori Kolmogorov complexity; C is plain complexity; superscripts denote oracles. A pointed perfect tree is a perfect binary tree computed by each of its paths. Turing invariance means x≡\_T y implies f(x)≡\_T f(y).

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Questions*. 2026. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/questions.html) Location: Part 1 of Martin’s conjecture, opening question.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1482, 1483, checked 6 October 2026: Still listed on the author’s current page; bounded searches found no answers.


<a id="q1574"></a>

## Q1574. Prescribe both finite presentation dimensions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.0.3) · **Collection** 16

For every pair of positive integers n,m, is there a structure with computable dimension n and punctual dimension m?

**Context.** Origin for question 1574: Conjecture 2.0.3 in the dissertation; finite punctual dimensions alone were constructed in the author’s CiE 2024 work. Setup for question 1574: Punctual presentations have domain N (or a finite initial segment) and primitive-recursive functions and relations. Punctual isomorphisms and their inverses are primitive recursive. Computable and punctual dimensions count presentations up to computable and punctual isomorphism, respectively.

**Source.** Ellen Hammatt. *Arriving on Time: Punctuality in Structures, Isomorphisms and 1-Decidability*. 2025. Advisor(s): Sasha Melnikov and Dan Turetsky. [primary source](https://openaccess.wgtn.ac.nz/articles/thesis/Arriving_on_Time_Punctuality_in_Structures_Isomorphisms_and_1-Decidability/28458236) Location: Conjecture 2.0.3, printed p. 15; Definitions 1.1.2–1.1.5, pp. 3–5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1574, checked 6 October 2026: The 2025 thesis states the joint-dimension conjecture. No later resolution located.

## Q1575. Decomposition from scattered lists

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.3) · **Collection** 16

Is CB_{N^N}≤\_W ScList_{N^N}?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1575, 1576, 1577: Vittorio Cipriani. Many Problems, Different Frameworks: Classification of Problems in Computable Analysis and Algorithmic Learning Theory. PhD, Università degli Studi di Udine, 2023. Advisors: Alberto Marcone; Luca San Mauro. Origin for questions 1575, 1576, 1577: 2022 preprint; also Cipriani’s Many Problems, Different Frameworks: Classification of Problems in Computable Analysis and Algorithmic Learning Theory, University of Udine PhD (2023); advisors Alberto Marcone and Luca San Mauro. Setup for questions 1575, 1576, 1577: Closed sets use negative-information names; points, Cauchy names. A list of S is (n,(b_i,x_i)\_{i∈N}), with S={x_i:b_i=1}, n=0 for infinite S and n=|S|+1 otherwise. List_X lists countable closed subsets of X. ScList_X lists A minus its perfect kernel; CB_X additionally returns that kernel as a closed set. C_X chooses a point of a nonempty closed set. ≤\_W is ordinary Weihrauch reducibility.

**Source.** Vittorio Cipriani, Alberto Marcone, Manlio Valenti. *The Weihrauch lattice at the level of Π¹₁-CA₀: the Cantor-Bendixson theorem*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2024.72) Location: Problem 6.3; thesis Question II.4.3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1575, 1576, 1577, checked 6 October 2026: Latest author version retains these problems; no later resolution located.

## Q1578. An everywhere arithmetically closed ultrafilter jump

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 16

Does some U make δ\_U(a) arithmetically closed for every Turing degree a?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1578, 1579, 1580: Joint work in thesis Chapter 7; questions retained in the later paper. Setup for questions 1578, 1579, 1580: U,V are nonprincipal ultrafilters on N. For degree a, δ\_U(a) consists of sets {j:{i:j∈X_i}∈U} from arrays (X_i) computable in a. Arithmetic closure means closure under Turing jump.

**Source.** Uri Andrews, Mingzhong Cai, David Diamondstone, Noah Schweber. *Limit computability and ultrafilters*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.3233/COM-170176) Location: Paper Question 2, §3; thesis Chapter 7, Question 2, p. 105.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1578, 1579, 1580, checked 6 October 2026: Explicitly open in the later paper; no subsequent resolution located. Its low-degree theorem settles a different thesis question.

## Q1583. A subgraph decision degree between consecutive LPO jumps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.2) · **Collection** 16

Do a computable graph G and an integer k≥1 exist with LPO^(k)<\_W S_G<\_W LPO^(k+1)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1583: see question 1575. Origin for question 1583: Question 5.2; also Cipriani’s Many Problems, Different Frameworks: Classification of Problems in Computable Analysis and Algorithmic Learning Theory, University of Udine PhD (2023), Question III.5.2; advisors Alberto Marcone and Luca San Mauro. Setup for question 1583: Graphs are countable, simple, undirected, with vertices in N; input vertex and edge sets are given by characteristic functions. S_G(H)=1 iff H contains a subgraph isomorphic to G, without requiring an induced copy. LPO(p)=1 iff p=0^ω. LPO^(k) denotes the k-fold Weihrauch jump, weakening input names by iterated limits; inequalities use ordinary Weihrauch reducibility.

**Source.** Vittorio Cipriani and Arno Pauly. *Embeddability of graphs and Weihrauch degrees*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0219061325500114) Location: Question 5.2, p. 38; definitions §2 and Definition 3.15; thesis Question III.5.2.

## Q1661. Linear size without a topological matching

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

For each fixed integer k≥3, does a constant C_k exist such that every n-vertex simple topological graph without k pairwise disjoint edges has at most C_kn edges?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1661, 1662: Edge bound: Pach–Tóth (2003). Complete-drawing question recalled after Aichholzer et al. (2022). Setup for questions 1661, 1662: A simple topological graph has point vertices and simple-arc edges in the plane, avoiding other vertices; two edges meet at most once, at a shared endpoint or proper crossing. Disjoint edges share no point.

**Source.** Jacob Fox; János Pach; Andrew Suk. *A structure theorem for pseudosegments and its applications*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jctb.2025.04.007) Location: §1, paragraph before Theorem 1.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1661, 1662, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found; SoCG 2026 reaffirms only Ω(√n) for complete drawings.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2312.01028) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.01028) · [3](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.SoCG.2026.63)

## Q1667. Optimal linear-order gap

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.4) · **Collection** 17

For each α, what is the least β such that every satisfiable Πα sentence of linear orders has a model A with SR(A)≤β?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1667, 1668, 1669: David Gonzalez. Countable Structures, Isomorphism, and Complexity. PhD, University of California, Berkeley, 2025. Advisor: Antonio Montalbán. Origin for questions 1667, 1668, 1669: Gonzalez’s Countable Structures, Isomorphism, and Complexity, Berkeley PhD 2025; advisor Antonio Montalbán. Faithful-completeness question: Gianluca Paolini. Setup for questions 1667, 1668, 1669: Ordinals and structures are countable. Σα,Πα are infinitary L_{ω₁ω} complexities. SR(A) is the least α admitting a Πα+1 Scott sentence, which characterizes A up to countable isomorphism. A Borel isomorphism-reduction is faithful when its range’s isomorphism-saturation is Borel.

**Source.** David Gonzalez; Matthew Harrison-Trainor. *Scott Spectral Gaps are Bounded for Linear Orderings*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12084v2) Location: Question 1.4; thesis §1.2, Question 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1667, 1668, 1669, checked 6 October 2026: Bounded searches found no resolution; the Boolean-algebra frontier is reaffirmed in Harrison-Trainor–Kim (2026).

**Further links.** [1](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/6pm9103z) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07166) · [3](https://math.berkeley.edu/publications/countable-structures-isomorphism-and-complexity)

## Q1670. Maximal back-and-forth types

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.9) · **Collection** 17

If M≤αN implies M≅N for every countable N, must M have a Πα+1 Scott sentence? Must it have a Πα Scott sentence?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1670: see question 1667. Origin for question 1670: Gonzalez thesis §3.1, Question 14; earlier related formulation in the spectral-gaps paper. Setup for question 1670: Structures share one countable language; ordinals are countable. M≤αN means every Πα sentence of L_{ω₁ω} true in M is true in N (α≥1). A Scott sentence characterizes its structure up to isomorphism among countable models.

**Source.** Ruiyuan Chen; David Gonzalez; Matthew Harrison-Trainor. *Optimal Syntactic Definitions of Back-and-Forth Types*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00893v2) Location: Question 1.9, p. 6.

## Q1671. Other scattered Σ-complexities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 17

Does there exist a countable scattered linear order L with SSC(L)=Σα for some α≥5 that is not the successor of a nonzero limit ordinal?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1671: see question 1667. Origin for question 1671: Gonzalez thesis §2.2, Question 12; related earlier Gonzalez–Rossegger question. Setup for question 1671: A countable linear order is scattered if Q does not embed into it. SSC is exact Scott-sentence complexity in the infinitary Σα,Πα,d-Σα hierarchy; d-Σα denotes a conjunction of a Σα and a Πα sentence.

**Source.** David Gonzalez; Matthew Harrison-Trainor; Meng-Che “Turbo” Ho. *Scott analysis, linear orders, and almost periodic functions*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/blms.70020) Location: Question 1, §4; thesis Question 12.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1671, checked 6 October 2026: Known Σλ+1 examples for nonzero limits λ do not settle other Σ-levels; no later resolution located.

## Q1672. Atomic pairs below ∅′

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.4) · **Collection** 17

Do two existentially closed groups with ∅′-computable atomic diagrams exist such that every finitely generated group embedding into both has computable word problem?

**Context.** Origin for question 1672: Thesis Chapter 1; revised journal §7–8. Setup for question 1672: Groups are countable. Existentially closed means every finite system of equations and inequations over G solvable in a supergroup is solvable in G. W(H) is a finitely generated group’s word problem.

**Source.** Isabella Scott. *On Effective Constructions of Existentially Closed Groups*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.10144) Location: Journal Question 8.4; thesis 1.7.0.4.

<a id="q1673"></a>

## Q1673. Ziegler ∀∃ theory

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 17

Is the ∀∃ fragment of the first-order theory of (D\*,≤\*) decidable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1673, 1674: Questions 6.1–6.2; closed ideals and their group correspondence originate with Martin Ziegler (1980). Setup for questions 1673, 1674: For subsets A,B⊆ω, A≤\*B means membership in A is enumerable from finite positive information about B, and nonmembership from finite positive information plus at most one negative fact. D\* is the resulting degree partial order. Groups are countable; existential closure is with respect to all group extensions.

**Source.** Josiah Jacobsen-Grocott; Steffen Lempp; Isabella Scott. *On the structure of the Ziegler degrees*. 2025. [primary source](https://people.math.wisc.edu/~slempp/papers/Ziegler.pdf) Location: Question 6.1, p. 13 (August 2026 copy).

## Q1767. Generic compact metrizable group

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.2) · **Collection** 18

Does S(U) contain a comeager topological-isomorphism class?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1767: Tamás Kátay. Generic behavior in various spaces of mathematical objects. PhD, Eötvös Loránd University, 2024. Advisors: Márton Elekes; Tamás Keleti. Origin for question 1767: Kátay, Generic behavior in various spaces of mathematical objects, ELTE thesis (2024; defended 2025), supervisors Márton Elekes and Tamás Keleti. Setup for question 1767: Let U=∏\_{n≥1}SU(n), with its compact product topology. ## Q1768. Small ultrapowers force small nonmeager sets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 18

Does ZFC prove KT(ℵ₁)⇒non(M)=ℵ₁?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1768, 1769, 1770: Tatsuya Goto. Goldstern’s principle and other applications of cardinal invariants of the continuum. PhD, Kobe University, 2025. Advisor: Jörg Brendle. Origin for questions 1768, 1769, 1770: Goto’s doctoral Chapter 5; advisor Jörg Brendle. First two questions originate in the 2021–2022 paper. Setup for questions 1768, 1769, 1770: KT(κ) means every pair A≡B of structures of size ≤κ in a countable first-order language has A^ω/U≅B^ω/U for some nonprincipal ultrafilter U on ω. M is the meager ideal of 2^ω; non(M) is the least size of a nonmeager set, cov(M) the least size of a meager cover.

**Source.** Tatsuya Goto. *Keisler’s Theorem and Cardinal Invariants*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.04438v3) Location: Question 6.1(1); thesis 5.6.1(1).

## Q1771. Larger continuum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

Is ZFC+GP(all)+c>ℵ₂ consistent?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1771, 1772, 1773, 1774: see question 1768. Earlier principle: Goldstern (1993). Setup for questions 1771, 1772, 1773, 1774: GP(Γ) asserts: in any Polish probability space (Y,μ), every A⊆ω^ω×Y in boldface Γ with μ-null sections A_x and x≤y pointwise⇒A_x⊆A_y has μ-null union ⋃\_x A_x. “all” imposes no definability restriction. c=2^ℵ₀; b,d are the unbounding and dominating numbers for eventual domination.

**Source.** Tatsuya Goto. *Goldstern’s principle about unions of null sets*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.08147v4) Location: 11.1(1); thesis 3.9.1(1).

<a id="q1775"></a>

## Q1775. Splitting games below closed-null uniformity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question A) · **Collection** 18

Does ZFC prove s_\*≤non(E)? Does it prove the stronger s_\*\*≤non(E)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1775, 1776, 1777: see question 1768. Origin for questions 1775, 1776, 1777: Goto’s doctoral Chapter 7; advisor Jörg Brendle. Four-author paper supplies the refined \*\* game. Setup for questions 1775, 1776, 1777: Fix A⊆P(ω). Players alternately choose bits i_n,j_n, producing X={n:i_n=1},Y={n:j_n=1}. In game \*, II wins if X is finite or Y∈A splits X (both X∩Y,X∖Y infinite). Game \*\* changes only the both-finite outcome to I’s win. s_\* and s_\*\* are minimum |A| for which I has no winning strategy. E is the ideal generated by closed null sets; non(E) is its uniformity, add(N) the null ideal’s additivity, d the eventual dominating number.

**Source.** Jorge Antonio Cruz Chapital; Tatsuya Goto; Yusuke Hayashi; Takashi Yamazoe. *Game-theoretic variants of splitting number*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19556) Location: §4, Question A; \* version thesis 7.6.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1775, 1776, 1777, checked 6 October 2026: 2026 journal publication; May 2026 evasion follow-up does not settle these three questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00153-026-01015-5) · [2](https://t-goto.jp/phdthesis.pdf) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26611) · [4](https://t-goto.jp/)

<a id="q1778"></a>

## Q1778. Exact reaping-game cardinal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question A) · **Collection** 18

Does ZFC prove r_game=max{r_σ,d}?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1778: see question 1768. Origin for question 1778: Goto’s doctoral §7.4, supervised by Jörg Brendle; original three-author paper, 2023. Setup for question 1778: Fix ℱ⊆[ω]^ω. I chooses increasing n_k; II replies i_k∈{0,1}. II wins if some A∈ℱ has A∩{n_k}={n_k:i_k=1} and either A⊆\*{n_k} or A⊆\*(ω∖{n_k}). r_game is the least |ℱ| for which I has no winning strategy. r_σ is the least size of a family containing, for every sequence (X_n)⊆P(ω), an infinite A almost contained in X_n or its complement for each n. Here ⊆\* means modulo finitely many elements; d is the eventual dominating number.

**Source.** Jorge Antonio Cruz Chapital; Tatsuya Goto; Yusuke Hayashi. *Game-theoretic variants of cardinal invariants*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12136v3) Location: §4, Question A; thesis 7.6.4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1778, checked 6 October 2026: Current arXiv retains Question A; 2025 proceedings verified. No later resolution located.

<a id="q1779"></a>

## Q1779. Strong-null versus universal-null cofinality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.2) · **Collection** 18

Does ZFC prove cof(SN)≤cof(UN)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1779, 1780: Postdoctoral paper; explicit questions in September 2026 revision. Setup for questions 1779, 1780: On Cantor space, UN consists of sets with outer measure zero for every atomless finite Borel measure; N is the usual null ideal. SN denotes strong measure zero: covers exist with any prescribed positive diameter bounds. For an ideal I, add(I),cov(I),non(I),cof(I) are respectively the least sizes of a subfamily whose union escapes I, a cover, a set outside I, and an inclusion-cofinal subfamily.

**Source.** Tatsuya Goto. *Forcing and Cardinal Invariants of Universally Null Sets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12936v3) Location: Question 6.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1779, 1780, checked 6 October 2026: Version 3, 7 September 2026, explicitly retains all four; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.12936v3) · [2](https://t-goto.jp/)

<a id="q1781"></a>

## Q1781. Exact zero-evasion number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 18

Does ZFC prove e₀=min{e,b}, equivalently e₀≤b?

**Context.** Origin for question 1781: Paper-only question; discovered through the Goto–Brendle collaboration network, without inferring doctoral supervision. Setup for question 1781: A predictor π=(D,(π\_n)) has infinite D⊆ω and π\_n:ω^n→ω. It predicts f∈ω^ω if π\_n(f↾n)=f(n) for all but finitely many n∈D. Zero-prediction reverses this relation exactly on eventually-zero functions. e and e₀ are the least sizes of families that no single predictor predicts or zero-predicts, respectively. b is the eventual-domination unbounding number.

**Source.** Takashi Yamazoe. *Evasion numbers via zero-prediction*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26611) Location: §4, Question 1; Definitions 2.1–2.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1781, checked 6 October 2026: May 2026 v1 explicitly leaves the equality open; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.26611) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/takashi-yamazoe/publications)

<a id="q1831"></a>

## Q1831. Coproducts preserve continuous sections

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.22) · **Collection** 19

For an arbitrary family of weak-split extensions, is their coordinatewise direct-sum sequence weak-split when all three sums carry topological-abelian-group coproduct topologies?

**Context.** Origin for question 1831: Authors’ selected questions in §5.3. Setup for question 1831: An extension 0→B→X→A→0 of topological abelian groups (no separation axiom assumed) is topologically exact: B has the subspace topology and A the quotient topology. It is weak-split if X→A has a continuous section s with s(0)=0, not required to be a homomorphism. G♯ denotes the abelian group G with its Bohr topology, the coarsest topology making all homomorphisms G→R/Z continuous.

**Source.** María V. Ferrer, Salvador Hernández-Muñoz and Luis Javier Hernández-Paricio. *Weak-split extensions of topological Abelian groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.08069) Location: Question 5.22, p. 26.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1831, checked 6 October 2026: the selected questions remain source-stated; bounded later searches found no resolutions.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.3934/era.2026078) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08069)

## Q1869. Least continuum for vanishing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 19

What is the least possible 2^ℵ₀ in a model of ZFC where limⁿA=0 for every integer n>0?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1869: Matteo Casarosa, Set theory of derived functors, Bologna/Paris Cité (2025); supervisors: Martino Lupini, Alessandro Vignati. Origin for question 1869: Doctoral Chapter 2; first question: Bergfalk–Lambie-Hanson (2021). <a id="q1872"></a>

## Q1872. Projective Polish p-groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.8) · **Collection** 19

Characterize the projective objects of PAb(p). Does this category have enough projectives?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1872, 1873: see question 1869. Origin for questions 1872, 1873: First posed in 2024; doctoral Chapter 4. Setup for questions 1872, 1873: Fix a prime p. PAb(p) and proTorPAb consist of Polish abelian groups obtainable as inverse limits of countable discrete p-groups and torsion groups, respectively. Morphisms are continuous homomorphisms. Projectivity means lifting through surjective morphisms within the category; enough projectives means every object admits such a surjection from a projective object.

**Source.** Matteo Casarosa; Alessandro Codenotti; Martino Lupini. *Homological algebra of pro-Lie Polish abelian groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://tac.mta.ca/tac/volumes/45/17/45-17.pdf) Location: Journal Problem 4.8, p. 646; preprint Problem 4.7; thesis Problem 4.80.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1872, 1873, checked 6 October 2026: March 2026 journal explicitly retains both; the locally compact subcategories are already settled.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13444v2) · [2](https://amsdottorato.unibo.it/id/eprint/12549/1/Tesi%20con%20frontespizio%20Bologna.pdf) · [3](https://phd.unibo.it/matematica/en/agenda/defense-casarosa) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25474)

## Q1875. Finite-type degree spectrum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.2) · **Collection** 19

For every infinite finitely generated recursively presented G, is G non-virtually-free exactly when its nonempty SFTs realize every Π⁰₁ Medvedev degree?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1875, 1876: Nicanor Carrasco-Vargas, Subshifts on groups and computable analysis, Pontificia Universidad Católica de Chile (2024); advisor Cristóbal Rojas; co-advisor Sebastián Barbieri. Origin for questions 1875, 1876: Doctoral Chapter 5. Setup for questions 1875, 1876: Finite-alphabet subshifts are closed shift-invariant sets. SFTs forbid finitely many patterns; sofic shifts are SFT factors. Compute on free-group pullbacks; effective means Π⁰₁ there. P≤\_M Q means one oracle algorithm maps every q∈Q into P. Π⁰₁ degrees have nonempty effectively closed representatives; m(X) denotes X’s degree.

**Source.** Sebastián Barbieri; Nicanor Carrasco-Vargas. *Medvedev degrees of subshifts on groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12777v2) Location: Conjecture 6.2 (p. 21); thesis 5.48.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1875, 1876, checked 6 October 2026: Cocompact-Fuchsian SFT spectrum settled September 2026; general questions remain.

<a id="q1878"></a>

## Q1878. Effective extensions with decidable domino problem

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.2) · **Collection** 19

If G has decidable word and domino problems, is every G-wEDS a topological factor of a G-EDS?

**Context.** Origin for question 1878: The general doctoral-era question is refuted here; this is the authors’ explicitly retained decidable-domino case. Setup for question 1878: All groups are finitely generated; actions are continuous on nonempty zero-dimensional compact metrizable spaces. A wEDS is conjugate to an inverse limit of effective finite-alphabet G-subshifts with factor bonding maps, without a uniform computability requirement. An EDS is conjugate to an action by computable generator homeomorphisms on a Π⁰₁ subset of Cantor space. A factor map is continuous, equivariant and surjective.

**Source.** Sebastián Barbieri; Leo Poirier. *A remark on inverse limits of effective subshifts*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00183v2) Location: Question 5.2, p. 9; definitions 2.6, 2.10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1878, checked 6 October 2026: December 2025 revision retains this question; March 2026 publication and later search reveal no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1112/blms.70277)


<a id="q1879"></a>

## Q1879. Many-one spectrum of inclusion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.3) · **Collection** 19

For fixed d≥2, which many-one degrees occur for {X:Y⊆X} as the fixed Z^d-SFT Y varies?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1879, 1880: Questions first posed in the September 2025 paper; revised February 2026. Setup for questions 1879, 1880: Fix d; all alphabets are finite. An SFT is a Z^d-subshift specified by finitely many forbidden patterns. Y is fixed; only X is input, encoded by such a finite list (same alphabet for inclusion). L(Y) is its finite-pattern language. An embedding Y↪X is an injective continuous shift-commuting map. Many-one reductions are computable; they need not be uniform in Y.

**Source.** Nicanor Carrasco-Vargas; Benjamin Hellouin de Menibus; Rémi Pallen. *Parametrized complexity of relations between multidimensional subshifts*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24343v2) Location: Question 4.3, p. 7; Theorem 4.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1879, 1880, checked 6 October 2026: All retained in v2; current author publication lists and bounded searches reveal no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://nicanorcarrascovargas.github.io/research/) · [2](https://perso.lisn.upsaclay.fr/hellouin/index_en.html)

## Q1881. Optimal hybrid interpolant size

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

What is the optimal worst-case minimum Craig-interpolant size for H_{@,U} input pairs of total size n that admit one, between the known triply and fourfold exponential bounds?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1881, 1882: New 2026 paper questions; discovered through Kołodziejski’s verified Warsaw doctorate under Klin. Setup for questions 1881, 1882: H is multimodal logic with nominals denoting singleton states. H_{@,U} adds evaluation @a at the state named a and universal modality [U]. A Craig interpolant χ for φ→ψ uses only shared nonlogical symbols and satisfies φ⊨χ⊨ψ, over arbitrary Kripke models. Size counts formula-tree symbols.

**Source.** Jean Christoph Jung; Jędrzej Kołodziejski; Frank Wolter. *Computation and Size of Interpolants for Hybrid Modal Logics*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15821v2) Location: Introduction pp. 3–4; Theorem 28; §8, p. 29.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1881, 1882, checked 6 October 2026: May 2026 full version and LICS 2026 retain these; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.LICS.2026.60)
