## Q1883. Binary grades

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

What is the exact complexity of deciding whether two graded μML formulas, with grades encoded in binary, admit an ungraded ML separator?

**Context.** Origin for question 1883: August 2026 journal questions; expanded September 2025 preprint. Setup for question 1883: ML is basic multimodal logic and μML its positive least/greatest fixed-point extension, over arbitrary Kripke models. A separator ψ of φ,φ′ satisfies φ⊨ψ⊨¬φ′ and may use their union signature. Formula size is tree size; DAG-size counts distinct subformulae. Graded modalities count distinct successors satisfying a formula.

**Source.** Jean Christoph Jung; Jędrzej Kołodziejski. *The Complexity of Defining and Separating Fixpoint Formulae in Modal Logic*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24583v5) Location: §7 opening limitation and §8.3, p. 6:36; unary baseline Theorem 7.5.

<a id="q1968"></a>

## Q1968. G-delta maximal cofinitary groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 20

Does a Gδ maximal cofinitary group exist?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1968, 1969, 1970, 1971: see question 1973. Origin for questions 1968, 1969, 1970, 1971: Orbit question: Kastermans (2009); other questions: Mejak–Schrittesser, §4. Setup for questions 1968, 1969, 1970, 1971: G≤S∞ is cofinitary if each nonidentity permutation has finitely many fixed points; finitely periodic if each nonidentity cyclic subgroup has finitely many finite orbits. Maximality is among subgroups with the corresponding property. Descriptive complexity uses the inherited Baire-space topology. k-orbits use the diagonal action on injective k-tuples.

**Source.** Severin Mejak; David Schrittesser. *Definability of maximal cofinitary groups*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.05318v1) Location: Question 4.1, p. 20; thesis Part III, p. 54.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1968, 1969, 1970, 1971, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found. Schembecker’s finite direct products yield Σ⁰₂ groups, leaving Gδ existence open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24318) · [2](https://www.math.ku.dk/bibliotek/arkivet/phd-theses/phd23sm.pdf)

## Q1972. Coanalytic orthogonal families after Laver forcing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.8) · **Collection** 20

For a real a and x Laver-generic over L[a], can L[a][x] contain a Π¹₁[a] maximal orthogonal family in P(2^ω)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1972: see question 1973. Origin for question 1972: Schrittesser–Törnquist (2018), Open problem 1; restated in Mejak’s thesis. Setup for question 1972: P(2^ω) is the Polish space of Borel probability measures. An orthogonal family consists of pairwise mutually singular measures and is maximal under inclusion.

**Source.** Severin Mejak. *Orthogonality of measures and states*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.02767v5) Location: Question 3.8, p. 19; parameter-explicit thesis conclusion p. 84.

## Q1975. Large definable tight madness above bounding

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 20

Is b&lt;a consistent with a Π¹₂ tight mad family of size a?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1975, 1976: see question 1974. Origin for questions 1975, 1976: First: Friedman–Zdomskyy (2010), Question 18; doctoral Questions 3.6.2 and 3.6.4. Setup for questions 1975, 1976: a is the least infinite mad-family size; b the least size of an eventually unbounded subset of ω^ω; s the least size of a family splitting every infinite subset of ω; c=2^ℵ₀. For mad A, I(A) is the ideal generated by A and finite sets. Tight means every countable B⊆P(ω)∖I(A) admits a∈A meeting every b∈B infinitely.

**Source.** Vera Fischer; Julia Millhouse. *Strong Projective Witnesses*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08718v1) Location: Question 6.1, p. 37; thesis Question 3.6.2, p. 132.

## Q1977. Closed maximal eventually different permutations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6) · **Collection** 20

Is there a closed maximal eventually different family of permutations, as a subset of ω^ω?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1977, 1978: see question 1974. Origin for questions 1977, 1978: Permutation complexity: Question 6; cardinal equality restated in §3. Setup for questions 1977, 1978: Functions f,g∈ω^ω are eventually different when {n:f(n)=g(n)} is finite. a_e is the least size of an inclusion-maximal such family in ω^ω; a_p is the analogous minimum among permutations of ω, with maximality tested only against permutations.

**Source.** Julia Millhouse; Lukas Schembecker. *Coanalytic families of functions*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07222v2) Location: Question 6, p. 6.

<a id="q1979"></a>

## Q1979. Polynomial asymptotics of Presburger shattering

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.3) · **Collection** 20

For every definable S in every model of Presburger arithmetic, is π\_S(t) asymptotic to a polynomial?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1979, 1980, 1981: Aristomenis-Dionysios Papadopoulos, Model-theoretic considerations in finite combinatorics, Leeds (2024); advisors H. Dugald Macpherson and Pantelis Eleftheriou. Origin for questions 1979, 1980, 1981: January 2026 questions; interpretation question continues doctoral Question 5. Setup for questions 1979, 1980, 1981: For E⊆∏ᵢM^{dᵢ}, Zar(E) means: if E contains no product of r k-element sets for some k, then |E∩∏ᵢBᵢ|≤C∑ᵢ∏ⱼ≠ᵢ|Bⱼ| for all finite Bᵢ, with C depending on E. For a definable set system S, π\_S(t)=max_{|A|=t}|{A∩S:S∈S}|.

**Source.** Pantelis E. Eleftheriou; Aris Papadopoulos. *On the global linear Zarankiewicz problem*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03546v2) Location: Question 9.3, p. 60.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1979, 1980, 1981, checked 6 October 2026: January 2026 revision states all three; no subsequent resolution found through 6 October.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Exact shatter functions in every Presburger model for unions of at most r intervals (Σ\_j binom(t+1,2j) ~ t^(2r)/(2r)!) and for independent residue-class interval families, with leading constants; each π\_φ(t) is computable and model-independent for parameter-free φ. Scope: Arbitrary definable families with coupled parameters, residues and coordinates remain open. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/35815/1/mtcifc.pdf) · [2](https://arispapadopoulos.github.io/) · [3](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/35815/)

<a id="q1982"></a>

## Q1982. Finite multiplicative stabilization forces strong compactness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

If s^{n+1}=s^n for every s∈S and some fixed positive integer n, must Φ⊨\_Sψ imply Φ₀⊨\_Sψ for some finite Φ₀⊆Φ?

**Context.** Origin for question 1982: Section 7 conjecture, after the finite and lattice cases. Setup for question 1982: S is a commutative absorptive semiring (s+st=s), naturally ordered as a complete lattice; both operations preserve nonempty-chain suprema and infima. Infinite sums/products are suprema/infima of finite subsums/subproducts. Model-defining literal valuations give exactly one of L,¬L value 0; FO uses +,· for ∨,∧ and sums/products for ∃,∀, with Boolean equality. Φ⊨\_Sψ means ∏\_{φ∈Φ}π(φ)≤π(ψ) for every such interpretation. Here Φ is a set of first-order sentences and ψ is a sentence.

**Source.** Sophie Brinke; Anuj Dawar; Erich Grädel; Lovro Mrkonjić; Matthias Naaf. *Compactness in Semiring Semantics*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.13) Location: Section 7, p. 13:16; Definitions 4 and 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1982, checked 6 October 2026: CSL 2026 explicitly retains this; later preservation results do not settle it. No resolution found through 6 October.

**Further links.** [1](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol363-csl2026/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.13/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.13.pdf) · [2](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.172)


<a id="q1983"></a>

## Q1983. Join closure below a jump-traceable set

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.3) · **Collection** 20

For jump-traceable B, is {A:A≤\_SJT B} closed under join ⊕?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1983, 1984: Section 4 questions and conjecture. Setup for questions 1983, 1984: A≤\_SJT B means every positive computable nondecreasing unbounded h admits uniformly B-c.e. finite T_n with |T_n|≤h(n) tracing J^A(n) whenever defined; J^A is universal partial A-computable. Jump-traceable means one computably bounded c.e. trace exists. SJT-degrees use mutual reducibility. K-trivial means K(A↾n)≤K(n)+O(1), with prefix-free complexity K; MLR^A denotes A-relative Martin-Löf randomness.

**Source.** Noam Greenberg; André Nies; Daniel Turetsky. *Characterising SJT reducibility*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23572v2) Location: Question 4.3, p. 19.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1983, 1984, checked 6 October 2026: June 2026 revision/journal retain these; no later resolution found through 6 October.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2026.10228) · [2](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~greenberg/Papers/94-GreenbergNiesTuretsky_SJT_reducibility_2025.pdf)

<a id="q2067"></a>

## Q2067. Removing coefficient restrictions from simulation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.1) · **Collection** 21

Does CP quasipolynomially simulate unrestricted SP?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2067, 2068, 2069: Toronto PhD (2021); advisor Toniann Pitassi. Origin for questions 2067, 2068, 2069: Thesis Problems 4.1–4.2; branch-and-cut collaboration (2021). Setup for questions 2067, 2068, 2069: Inputs are unsatisfiable CNFs in their standard Boolean-inequality encoding. CP uses nonnegative linear combinations and integer rounding. SP is an integer-split branching tree with LP-infeasible leaves. SP\* bounds coefficient magnitudes and CP\* bounds each coefficient vector’s ℓ₁-norm by n^{(log n)^{O(1)}}; n counts input variables. Proof size is binary encoding length. Simulation bounds the minimum target proof size polynomially or quasipolynomially in the minimum source proof size.

**Source.** Noah Fleming. *The Proof Complexity of Integer Programming*. 2021. [primary source](https://noahrfleming.github.io/papers/Thesis.pdf) Location: Problem 4.1, p. 82.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2067, 2068, 2069, checked 6 October 2026: June journal discussion retains polynomial and coefficient-preserving questions; no resolution of unrestricted quasipolynomial simulation found.

**Further links.** [1](https://theoryofcomputing.org/articles/v022a002/v022a002.pdf)

<a id="q2070"></a>

## Q2070. Extension preservation at clique-width three

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

Does {G:cliquewidth(G)≤3} have EP?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2070, 2071, 2072: see question 2074. Origin for questions 2070, 2071, 2072: Conclusion §5; bounded-ladder question also thesis Question 5. Setup for questions 2070, 2071, 2072: Graphs are finite, simple and undirected. EP means every first-order sentence preserved by induced embeddings between class members is equivalent there to an existential sentence. ladder(G) is the largest n for which distinct a₁,…,aₙ,b₁,…,bₙ satisfy E(aᵢ,bⱼ) iff i≤j; edges within either side are unrestricted.

**Source.** Ioannis Eleftheriadis. *Extension Preservation on Dense Graph Classes*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2025.7) Location: §5, CSL p. 7:15 (arXiv p. 20).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2070, 2071, 2072, checked 6 October 2026: these boundary cases remain source-stated open; no later resolution located.

For every k∈N, does {G:cliquewidth(G)≤k} have HP?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2073, 2074: Cambridge PhD (2025); advisor Anuj Dawar. Origin for questions 2073, 2074: Thesis §6.4; extends Dawar–Eleftheriadis (2024). Setup for questions 2073, 2074: Graphs are finite, simple, undirected. HP (respectively EP) means every first-order sentence preserved by homomorphisms (respectively induced embeddings) within the class is equivalent there to an existential-positive (respectively existential) sentence. Excluding a set of minors means excluding each listed graph as a minor.

**Source.** Ioannis Eleftheriadis. *Model-theoretic aspects of structural and algorithmic graph theory*. 2025. [primary source](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/bitstreams/d8aebd4b-e8b2-452a-a276-094ce73af2a9/download) Location: Question 4, p. 170.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2073, 2074, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found for these exact class-relative preservation questions.

## Q2075. Almost-optimal tuple-type counts

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3) · **Collection** 21

For monadically stable C and every ε>0, is |{{ā∈A^{|x̄|}:G⊨φ(ā,b̄)}:b̄∈V(G)^{|ȳ|}}|=O(|A|^{|ȳ|+ε}) uniformly for G∈C?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2075: see question 2073. Origin for question 2075: 2023 preprint Conjecture 3; FOCS 2024; thesis Conjecture 2. Setup for question 2075: C is a class of finite graphs. Monadically stable means that arbitrary unary expansions cannot first-order interpret arbitrarily long linear orders. φ(x̄,ȳ) is any fixed first-order formula; A is a nonempty vertex subset. Constants in O may depend only on C,φ,ε.

## Q2076. Homogenizability survives taking cores

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 21

Is there a finitely homogenizable structure whose model-complete core is not finitely homogenizable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2076, 2077, 2078: First: Bodirsky (2020), Question (5). Others: this paper. Setup for questions 2076, 2077, 2078: Structures are countable relational. Finitely homogeneous=homogeneous with finite signature; finitely homogenizable=interdefinable with such a structure. Model-complete cores are homomorphically equivalent structures whose formulas are existential-positive modulo their theory. Interpretations/interdefinability use parameter-free first-order formulas. Lachlan’s class comprises ω-stable first-order reducts of finitely homogeneous structures. Binary means arities≤2.

**Source.** Manuel Bodirsky; Bertalan Bodor; Paolo Marimon. *Taking model-complete cores*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21278v2) Location: Question 1.1, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2076, 2077, 2078, checked 6 October 2026: July 2026 v2 retains these questions; no later resolution found through 6 October.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.sylvyanscombe.com/LAP2026/LAPtalksabstracts.html)

## Q2079. Strong modeling limits for stable graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.1) · **Collection** 21

Does every FO-convergent sequence from a monadically stable graph class have a strong modeling FO-limit?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2079, 2080: Conjectures 7.1–7.2 of this work. Setup for questions 2079, 2080: Sequences comprise finite structures. FO-convergence means convergence of satisfaction probabilities for every first-order formula under independent uniform vertex sampling. A modeling FO-limit is a standard Borel probability structure with all definable sets measurable and the limiting probabilities. Monadically stable/NIP means stability/NIP survives arbitrary unary expansions. A graph modeling is strong if aν(A)≤bν(B) whenever a,b∈N and measurable A,B have every A-vertex adjacent to at least a B-vertices and every B-vertex to at most b A-vertices.

**Source.** Samuel Braunfeld; Jaroslav Nešetřil; Patrice Ossona de Mendez. *Modeling FO-limits for monadically stable sequences*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08960v1) Location: Conjecture 7.1, p. 18.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2079, 2080, checked 6 October 2026: stable modeling existence is proved; these strengthenings remain unresolved in the sources searched.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10941)

## Q2081. Composing independent-defect valuations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.9) · **Collection** 21

If v=v̄∘w is henselian and both (K,w) and (Kw,v̄) are independent defect fields, must (K,v) be an independent defect field?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2081: Ramello: Münster PhD (2025); advisors Franziska Jahnke and Martin Hils. Origin for question 2081: Question 4.9; Ramello thesis Question 3.4.10. Setup for question 2081: For henselian (F,u) of residue characteristic p>0, put F′=F(ζ\_p), with ζ\_p a primitive pth root, in characteristic 0 and F′=F otherwise. It is an independent defect field if every degree-p Galois defect extension (L,u)/(F′,u) is independent: for nonidentity σ, {u((σx−x)/x):x∈L×}={γ∈uF′∪{∞}:γ>H} for a proper convex H with uF′/H having no least positive element.

**Source.** Margarete Ketelsen; Simone Ramello; Piotr Szewczyk. *Definable henselian valuations in positive residue characteristic*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2024.55) Location: Question 4.9 (arXiv v3 p. 11; journal p. 13); thesis Q3.4.10, p. 26.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2081, checked 6 October 2026: composition with a defectless factor is known; no unrestricted composition theorem found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06884) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15376) · [3](https://d-nb.info/1372173587/34) · [4](https://simoneramello.it/?page_id=408)

## Q2082. Finite-color promise compactness implies ultrafilters

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.2) · **Collection** 21

For every integer c≥6, is K_(K₃,K_c) equivalent to UL over ZF?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2082, 2083: Research following Bodor’s TU Dresden PhD (2021), CSP dichotomy for ω-categorical monadically stable structures; supervisor Manuel Bodirsky. Origin for questions 2082, 2083: Conjecture 6.2 and Question 6.3. Setup for questions 2082, 2083: For finite relational A→B, K_(A,B) says every structure I whose finite substructures map homomorphically to A maps to B. UL is the ultrafilter lemma; all equivalences are over ZF. K_c is the c-clique. T on {0,1} has ternary relation {001,010,100}; H₂ has all nonconstant binary-valued triples.

**Source.** Bertalan Bodor. *Equivalences of promise compactness principles*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08365v2) Location: Conjecture 6.2, p. 24; c≤5 settled by main theorem.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2082, 2083, checked 6 October 2026: May 2026 v2 retains both. September 2026 CSP compactness results concern equal templates, leaving these promise cases open.

<a id="q2168"></a>

## Q2168. Presburger complexity at Σ4

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 22

Does a Presburger group P with SC(P)=Σ4 exist?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Jason Block. Computability Theoretic Aspects of Profinite Groups and Models of Presburger Arithmetic. PhD, CUNY Graduate Center, 2025. Advisor: Russell Miller. Origin for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Questions 6.1, 6.4–6.5. Setup for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Structures use computable signatures. A Presburger group is a countable model of Th(Z,+,<,0,1). DgSp(A) is the set of Turing degrees of atomic diagrams of copies on N. SC denotes optimal infinitary Scott-sentence complexity in the Σα, Πα, d-Σα hierarchy. A is automorphically trivial if some finite F⊂A has every permutation fixing F pointwise as an automorphism.

**Source.** Jason Block. *Measuring the Complexity of Countable Presburger Models*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21118v3) Location: Question 6.1, §6.1, p. 29.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2168, 2169, 2170, checked 6 October 2026: June v3 retains these questions; no later solution found.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Yes. For any positive transcendental t, let W=Q[t]+span_Q{(t+n)^-1: n≥1} and P=W×Z, lexicographic with W more significant and 1=(0,1). P has a Σ4 Scott sentence and no Π4 Scott sentence, so SC(P)=Σ4. Scope: Full retained question; written proof (Part I) using Presburger quantifier elimination and Montalbán's orbit theorem. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/6293/)


<a id="q2169"></a>

## Q2169. Removing the discrete factor

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.4) · **Collection** 22

For every nonzero countable divisible ordered abelian group V, is DgSp(V)=DgSp(V×Z), with lexicographic order and distinguished 1=(0,1)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Jason Block. Computability Theoretic Aspects of Profinite Groups and Models of Presburger Arithmetic. PhD, CUNY Graduate Center, 2025. Advisor: Russell Miller. Origin for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Questions 6.1, 6.4–6.5. Setup for questions 2168, 2169, 2170: Structures use computable signatures. A Presburger group is a countable model of Th(Z,+,<,0,1). DgSp(A) is the set of Turing degrees of atomic diagrams of copies on N. SC denotes optimal infinitary Scott-sentence complexity in the Σα, Πα, d-Σα hierarchy. A is automorphically trivial if some finite F⊂A has every permutation fixing F pointwise as an automorphism.

**Source.** Jason Block. *Measuring the Complexity of Countable Presburger Models*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21118v3) Location: Question 6.4, §6.2, p. 29.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2168, 2169, 2170, checked 6 October 2026: June v3 retains these questions; no later solution found.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: DgSp(V)=DgSp(V×Z) whenever V has a least nonzero Archimedean class, which covers Archimedean V, finite Archimedean rank and finite dimension; plus a presentation-level criterion for computing the quotient. Scope: V without a least Archimedean class remains open. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b1-2026-10-07/B1-research-report-2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/6293/)

## Q2171. Scott ideals give full elementarity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 22

For every such G and Scott ideal I, must G_I be an elementary subgroup of G in the first-order group language?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2171: see question 2168. Origin for question 2171: §5, Question 3. Setup for question 2171: Let G≤S∞ be profinite and T_G={g↾n:g∈G,n∈N}. A Turing ideal I is nonempty, downward closed and closed under finite joins; require deg(T_G)∈I. Put G_I={g∈G:deg(g)∈I}. I is Scott when each a∈I has a PA degree relative to a in I.

## Q2173. Admissibility over extendable parameters

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 22

Is ∼Γ decidable when Γ comprises the weakly extendable formulas?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2173, 2174: Konstantinos Papafilippou. Modal Combinatorics: Unification, Fixed-Points, and Reflection. PhD, Ghent University, 2025. Promotor: Andreas Weiermann; Co-Promotors: David Fernández-Duque and Mojtaba Mojtahedi. Origin for questions 2173, 2174: §6; thesis §4.6. Setup for questions 2173, 2174: Work in IPC with disjoint infinite sets of variables and parameters. Substitutions fix parameters. A∼ΓB means: for every parameter-only E∈Γ and substitution θ, IPC⊢E→θ(A) implies IPC⊢E→θ(B). Weakly extendable means projective when parameters can also be substituted. Prime means A⊢B∨C implies A⊢B or A⊢C. A is par-projective if some parameter-fixing θ satisfies A⊢θ(a)↔a for every atom and θ(A) is equivalent to a parameter-only formula.

**Source.** Mojtaba Mojtahedi; Konstantinos Papafilippou. *Projectivity Meets Uniform Post-Interpolant: Classical and Intuitionistic Logic*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19525v2) Location: §6, Question 3(i); thesis Question 3, p. 66.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2173, 2174, checked 6 October 2026: retained in 2025 thesis; no subsequent answer found.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.aiml.net/volumes/volume15/26-MojtahediPapafilippou.pdf) · [2](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/01KJ77Q4EV3T1ECJCCR4FW67YH/file/01KJ77XH417R38HMXEVZRJW179.pdf) · [3](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/01KJ77Q4EV3T1ECJCCR4FW67YH)

<a id="q2175"></a>

## Q2175. Optimal GL fixed-point length

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

What is the sharp worst-case minimum size of ψ for length-n basic GL formulas φ(x) in which every x lies under a modality, between 2^{Ω(n)} and 2^{O(n log n)}?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2175: see question 2173. Origin for question 2175: §8, refining Theorems 6–7. Setup for question 2175: Formula size counts all symbols in a syntax tree. GL uses finite strict partial orders; Grz uses finite reflexive partial orders. A fixed-point formula ψ uses only atoms of φ other than x and satisfies φ(ψ)↔ψ throughout the chosen frame class.

**Source.** Konstantinos Papafilippou; David Fernández-Duque. *Exponential Lower Bounds on Definable Fixed Points*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2025.25) Location: §8, p. 25:18; Theorem 6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2175, checked 6 October 2026: The 2025 thesis retains the fixed-point size gap; no later improvement found.

**Further links.** [1](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/01KJ77Q4EV3T1ECJCCR4FW67YH/file/01KJ77XH417R38HMXEVZRJW179.pdf) · [2](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/01KJ77Q4EV3T1ECJCCR4FW67YH)


<a id="q2176"></a>

## Q2176. Optimal universal-tangle translation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

What is the optimal worst-case length of a target-language formula equivalent to an input formula of length n on all weakly transitive frames?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2176: see question 2173. Origin for question 2176: Theorem 9 and preceding discussion. Setup for question 2176: Frames satisfy R²⊆R∪Id. The target language has Boolean connectives, ◇, and TΓ=νx.∨\_{φ∈Γ}[(φ∧x∨◇(φ∧x))∧∧\_{ψ∈Γ∖{φ}}◇(ψ∧x)], for finite multisets Γ; x is fresh. Input is the modal μ-calculus with positive least/greatest fixed points. Size is total syntax-tree symbols, including tangle arguments.

**Source.** David Fernández-Duque; Konstantinos Papafilippou. *The Universal Tangle for Spatial Reasoning*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.03339v2) Location: Theorem 9 and preceding paragraph, p. 18; thesis p. 51.

<a id="q2177"></a>

## Q2177. Continuum many localisation values

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 22

Is it consistent that {d_h:h is increasing and cofinal in κ^κ} has cardinality 2^κ?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2177, 2178: Tristan van der Vlugt. Higher Baire Spaces: Cardinal Characteristics, Higher Reals & Bounded Spaces. PhD, Universität Hamburg, 2024. Advisors: Jörg Brendle and Benedikt Löwe. Origin for questions 2177, 2178: §4, Questions 4.1–4.2. Setup for questions 2177, 2178: Let κ be strongly inaccessible and h:κ→κ increasing and cofinal. An h-slalom φ has φ(α)⊆κ and |φ(α)|≤|h(α)|. Put d_h=min{|Φ|:every f∈κ^κ satisfies f(α)∈φ(α) eventually for some φ∈Φ}.

**Source.** Tristan van der Vlugt. *Separating Many Localisation Cardinals on the Generalised Baire Space*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2023.21) Location: Question 4.1, p. 1230.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2177, 2178, checked 6 October 2026: Benhamou–Switzer give restrictions, but do not settle these two questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07411) · [2](https://tvdvlugt.nl/dthesis.pdf) · [3](https://tvdvlugt.nl/writing.php)

## Q2179. Dominating forcing without Cohen reals

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.13) · **Collection** 22

Must every <κ-closed forcing that adds a dominating κ-real also add a κ-Cohen generic over the ground model?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2179, 2180: see question 2177. Origin for questions 2179, 2180: Questions 4.13–4.14; origins KKLW (2022), Halko–Shelah (2001). Setup for questions 2179, 2180: Let κ be uncountable regular with κ^{<κ}=κ. A dominating κ-real eventually bounds every ground-model f∈κ^κ. κ-Cohen forcing is 2^{<κ} ordered by extension. A set X⊆2^κ is κ-strong measure zero if each f∈κ^κ admits sα∈2^{f(α)} with X⊆⋃\_{α<κ}[sα].

**Source.** Tristan van der Vlugt. *The Horizontal Direction*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04471) Location: Question 4.13, p. 22.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2179, 2180, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution located for these precise formulations.

## Q2181. Nonuniform projective choice equivalence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 36) · **Collection** 22

For each n≥1, does ZF prove ACω(R;Π¹\_n)↔ACω(R;Σ¹\_{n+1})?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2181, 2182: Ned J. H. Wontner. Views from a Peak: Generalisations and Descriptive Set Theory. PhD, Universiteit van Amsterdam, 2023. Use the projective hierarchy obtained by projection and complement from closed sets. Let n≥1.

## Q2184. Capture at complete ineffability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.12) · **Collection** 22

Must every completely ineffable κ satisfy d_κ(∈\*)=2^κ?

**Context.** Origin for question 2184: Question 3.12, first part. Setup for question 2184: For regular uncountable κ, d_κ(∈\*) is the least size of a family of slaloms φ:κ→P(κ), |φ(α)|≤|α| for infinite α, which eventually capture every f∈κ^κ. A cardinal κ is completely ineffable if a nonempty upward-closed family R of stationary subsets of κ has: for every A∈R and coloring c:[A]²→2, some c-homogeneous B⊆A belongs to R.

**Source.** Tom Benhamou; Corey Bacal Switzer. *Eventual Capture on a Measurable Cardinal*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07411) Location: Question 3.12, p. 11.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2267, 2268: Tsinghua PhD 2026; supervisor Fenrong Liu; co-supervisor Nick Bezhanishvili. Origin for questions 2267, 2268: Chapter 7, p. 195. Setup for questions 2267, 2268: Tense logics are normal Boolean bimodal logics containing p→□\_+◇\_-p and p→□\_-◇\_+p; K_t is the least, S4_t its reflexive-transitive extension. Tabular means determined by one finite Kripke frame; Post-complete means consistent with no proper consistent extension. Kripke-complete means determined by its validating frames.

## Q2269. Counting finitely approximable pretabular logics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

S4_t is the logic of reflexive-transitive frames. A logic is tabular if determined by one finite frame, pretabular if consistent and non-tabular with every proper consistent extension tabular. FMP means determined by its finite validating frames. B=S4BP^{3,ω}\_{2,2} is the logic of reflexive-transitive frames whose strict chains have at most 3 points and whose successor and predecessor sets contain no 3-point antichain; zigzag length is unrestricted.

## Q2274. Entailment with nonemptiness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

Determine the complexity of deciding φ⊨ψ for input PL(NE) formulas φ,ψ, quantifying over all teams on their variables.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2274, 2275: Aleksi Ilari Anttila: Not Nothing: Nonemptiness in Team Semantics. Amsterdam PhD (2025); research supervisors Maria Aloni, Fan Yang. Formal promotores: Robert van Rooij, Maria Aloni; copromotor: Fan Yang. Origin for questions 2274, 2275: §6, p. 11. Setup for questions 2274, 2275: PL(NE) uses p,¬p,⊥,⊤, NE,∧,∨. Teams are sets of Boolean valuations. Literals hold pointwise; ⊥ means empty, ⊤ always holds, NE means nonempty, and ∧ requires both. X⊨φ∨ψ iff X=Y∪Z with Y⊨φ, Z⊨ψ, allowing overlap. Formula size counts syntax-tree symbols.

**Source.** Aleksi Anttila; Juha Kontinen; Fan Yang. *Complexity Results in Team Semantics: Nonemptiness Is Not So Complex*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08122v2) Location: §6, p. 11.

<a id="q2276"></a>

## Q2276. Tree bad sequences versus descending sequences

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.11) · **Collection** 23

Is BS_Tree≤\_WDS?

**Context.** Origin for question 2276: Questions 8.11,9.9–9.10. Setup for question 2276: Relations with domain contained in N and trees T⊆N^{&lt;N} use characteristic-function names. DS outputs an infinite descending sequence in an ill-founded linear order; BS outputs (x_n) with x_i≰x_j for i&lt;j in a non-well-quasi-order. BS_Tree restricts BS to prefix-ordered trees admitting infinite antichains. RT¹₂ maps c:N→2 to an infinite monochromatic subset. ≤\_W allows computable preprocessing, one oracle call, and postprocessing using the input; × is parallel product. Define f/g=max_{≤\_W}{h:h×g≤\_Wf}.

**Source.** Jun Le Goh; Arno Pauly; Manlio Valenti. *The weakness of finding descending sequences in ill-founded linear orders*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.10160) Location: Question 8.11, p. 28.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2276, checked 6 October 2026: final journal PDF retains all three; no later resolution located.

## Q2277. Defining the enumeration skip

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 23

Can one parameter-free first-order formula in the order language define b=a^♦ for all enumeration degrees a,b?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2277, 2278: §6, p. 37. Setup for questions 2277, 2278: A≤\_eB means A=Γ(B) for a computably enumerable enumeration operator. Fix an effective list Γ\_i; define A^♦=N∖⊕\_iΓ\_i(A), inducing the skip a^♦. A degree is total if it contains A⊕(N∖A), cototal if it contains B≤\_eN∖B, graph-cototal if it contains the complement of a total function’s graph. Arithmetic/Π⁰₂ degrees contain representatives of that complexity.

**Source.** Uri Andrews; Hristo A. Ganchev; Rutger Kuyper; Steffen Lempp; Joseph S. Miller; Alexandra A. Soskova; Mariya I. Soskova. *On cototality and the skip operator in the enumeration degrees*. 2019. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1090/tran/7604) Location: Question 6.1, p. 37.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2277, 2278, checked 6 October 2026: current author manuscript retains these; bounded searches found no resolution.

## Q2279. Pigeonhole hierarchy implies cohesiveness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.1) · **Collection** 23

Is there an integer n≥1 such that RCA₀+Σ⁰\_n-Subset proves COH?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2279, 2280, 2281: Ludovic Patey: The reverse mathematics of Ramsey-type theorems. Paris-Diderot PhD (2016); advisors Laurent Bienvenu and Hugo Herbelin. Origin for questions 2279, 2280, 2281: Questions 9.1,9.3–9.4, p. 61. Setup for questions 2279, 2280, 2281: Over RCA₀, Σ⁰\_n-Subset asserts that every Σ⁰\_n formula φ(x), allowing set parameters, has an infinite set H on which φ is constant. COH asserts that every sequence (R_i) has infinite C with each C∩R_i or C∖R_i finite. IΣ⁰₂ and BΣ⁰₂ are induction and bounding for Σ⁰₂ formulas. Π¹₁-conservative means adding no Π¹₁ sentence over the indicated base.

**Source.** Quentin Le Houérou; Ludovic Levy Patey; Ahmed Mimouni. *The reverse mathematics of the pigeonhole hierarchy*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01236v2) Location: Question 9.1, p. 61.

<a id="q2338"></a>

## Q2338. Universal minimal fibers in group extensions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.1) · **Collection** 24

If M(H) and M(K) are metrizable, must each fiber π^{-1}({y}), y∈M(K), be H-equivariantly homeomorphic to M(H)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2338, 2339, 2340: Authors’ Questions 7.1, 7.5, 7.6, originally 2019. Setup for questions 2338, 2339, 2340: Groups are Polish. M(G) is the universal minimal compact Hausdorff G-flow: every orbit is dense and every minimal compact G-flow is a continuous equivariant image. G is uniquely ergodic if M(G) has exactly one invariant Borel probability. For an exact sequence 1→H→G→K→1, H is closed normal, K=G/H, and π:M(G)→M(K) is the canonical factor map.

**Source.** Colin Jahel; Andy Zucker. *Topological dynamics of Polish group extensions*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.04901) Location: Question 7.1, arXiv p. 13

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2338, 2339, 2340, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no matching resolution; bounded-negative evidence.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejc.2025.104263)

## Q2368. Hypersmooth canonization of analytic relations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 24

For every analytic equivalence relation E on E∞, is there hypersmooth F≤\_B E such that every Borel I(E∞)-positive B contains Borel I(E∞)-positive C⊆B with E↾C≤\_B F?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2368: Allison Wang. Complexity in Countable Borel Equivalence Relations and Topological Ramsey Spaces. PhD, Carnegie Mellon University, 2026. Advisor: Clinton Conley. Origin for question 2368: Preprint 2022 §1.4, Problems 2–3; journal 2023. Setup for question 2368: For a closed topological Ramsey space R with countable approximations, I(R) is its Ellentuck-meager ideal; Borel refers to its Polish topology. E₀ is eventual equality on 2^ω; ≤\_B is Borel reducibility. E∞ denotes partitions of N into infinitely many blocks, ordered by coarsening. Its basic Ellentuck sets fix a finite initial segment and a coarsening bound. Hypersmooth means an increasing countable union of smooth Borel equivalence relations.

**Source.** Aristotelis Panagiotopoulos; Allison Wang. *Every CBER is smooth below the Carlson–Simpson generic partition*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.14224v1) Location: Problem 3, p. 4.

## Q2369. Intermediate hereditary model checking

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Assuming P≠NP, does some fixed FO sentence φ have coNP-intermediate Her(φ), under polynomial-time many-one reductions?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2369, 2370, 2371, 2372: §6; preprint 2024. Setup for questions 2369, 2370, 2371, 2372: Her(φ) comprises finite nonempty, finite-signature relational structures all of whose nonempty induced substructures satisfy φ. CSP(B) asks whether an input finite structure maps homomorphically to fixed B. Negative means all atoms negated in prenex CNF; connected means each negated clause’s canonical database is connected after identifying equality variables.

**Source.** Manuel Bodirsky; Santiago Guzmán-Pro. *Hereditary First-Order Logic: the Tractable Quantifier Prefix Classes*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.6) Location: §6(1), p. 6:16.

## Q2373. Finite promise witnesses for FPC failure

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.7) · **Collection** 24

If A is a reduct of a countable finitely bounded homogeneous structure and CSP(A) is not FPC-definable, must A pp-construct a finite-domain PCSP not FPC-definable?

**Context.** Origin for question 2373: Questions 5.7–5.8. Setup for question 2373: CSP(A) asks finite-input homomorphism existence into fixed A. FPC is inflationary fixed-point logic with counting. A pp-constructs (S₁,S₂) if it pp-interprets a structure homomorphically equivalent to C with S₁→C→S₂; PCSP(S₁,S₂) separates inputs mapping to S₁ from those not mapping to S₂. Phylogeny templates are finite-signature FO reducts of the homogeneous binary-tree leaf structure (L;xy|z), where xy|z means the common ancestor of x,y lies strictly below that of x,z.

**Source.** Michael Pinsker; Jakub Rydval; Moritz Schöbi; Christoph Spiess; Paul Winkler. *Three Fundamental Questions in Modern Infinite-Domain Constraint Satisfaction*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06621v4) Location: Question 5.7, p. 37.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2373, checked 6 October 2026: retained in inspected version; no later resolution located.

## Q2374. Classify quantifier-free dependence checking

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Classify the data complexity of model checking every fixed quantifier-free dependence formula. In particular, is every such problem in P or NP-complete?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2374, 2375: §6, items 1 and 3. Setup for questions 2374, 2375: A team T is a finite set of assignments into a finite structure. dep(x̄,y) requires any two assignments agreeing on x̄ to agree on y. FO literals hold pointwise; ∧ requires both formulas; T⊨φ∨ψ means T=T₁∪T₂ with T₁⊨φ,T₂⊨ψ. Quantifier-free dependence formulas use these constructs.

**Source.** Nicolas Fröhlich; Phokion G. Kolaitis; Arne Meier. *Disjunctions of Two Dependence Atoms*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.10) Location: §6(1), p. 10:16.

## Q2376. Exact complexity of DFA primality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

What is the exact computational complexity of deciding whether an input DFA is prime?

**Context.** Origin for question 2376: Kupferman–Mosheiff introduced the problem; Martens CSL 2026 §9 asks to close its complexity gap. Setup for question 2376: A complete DFA A is composite if L(A)=⋂\_{i=1}^t L(A_i) for finitely many complete DFAs each smaller than A’s minimal equivalent DFA; otherwise it is prime. Alphabet and transition tables are explicit finite input.

**Source.** Daniel Alexander Spenner. *Deciding DFA-Primality Is NP-Hard*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.192) Location: §1, p. 192:2; Martens§9.

## Q2377. Smash-product composition coherence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

In Book HoTT, is the smash-product left adjoint X∧−:U\*→U\* 2-coherent for every pointed X, with right adjoint X→\*−?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2377, 2378: HoTT/UF 2025 slides p. 12; CSL 2026 §8; expanded 2026 §8. Setup for questions 2377, 2378: In Book HoTT, a reflective subuniverse U_L has reflector L, unit η\_X:X→LX, and equivalences (LX→Y)≃(X→Y) for Y∈U_L. On A/U it induces L_A(X,s)=(LX,η\_X∘s), left adjoint to inclusion of L-local A-types. 2-coherence means, for every composable h₂,h₃ and suitable h₁, the paths ψ(h₁)∘h₂∘h₃=ψ(h₁∘L(h₂∘h₃)) produced by repeated hom-equivalence naturality and by L’s composition witness agree, including associators. U\* denotes pointed types; smash and pointed-map functors have their standard Book HoTT meanings.

**Source.** Perry Hart. *On Left Adjoints Preserving Colimits in Homotopy Type Theory*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28473) Location: §8, p. 20; Definition 5.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2377, 2378, checked 6 October 2026: retained in inspected version; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.20)

## Q2379. Two clocks in invariant-free networks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Is it decidable whether there exist a parameter valuation, a finite number of copies, and a finite run reaching an input target location in at least one copy?

**Context.** Origin for question 2379: §7 Perspectives, extending Theorem 20. Setup for question 2379: A network consists of any positive number of identical copies of an input finite timed-automaton template. Each has two nonnegative real clocks initially zero, advancing at common speed; transitions reset chosen clocks. Guards are conjunctions x⋈(∑a_i p_i+d), with integer coefficients, ⋈∈{<,≤,=,≥,>}, and shared parameters p_i∈N. A transition may additionally require another process at one specified location. All copies start in the initial location; no location invariants.

**Source.** Étienne André; Swen Jacobs; Engel Lefaucheux. *Parametric Disjunctive Timed Networks*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.31) Location: §7, p. 31:17.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2379, checked 6 October 2026: retained in inspected version; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04991v1)

## Q2380. Sparsify strong flip-flatness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 24

Is every strongly flip-flat class of finite graphs structurally uniformly almost-wide?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2380: Ioannis Eleftheriadis. Model-theoretic aspects of structural and algorithmic graph theory. PhD, University of Cambridge, 2025. Advisor: Anuj Dawar. Origin for question 2380: Earlier: Eleftheriadis’s 2025 thesis, Question 6 (uniform version); current paper Conjecture 2. Setup for question 2380: C is strongly flip-flat if ∃s ∀r ∃N_r ∀m,G∈C,A⊆V(G), |A|≥N_r(m) implies some ≤s adjacency-complementations between vertex-set pairs leave an m-subset of A pairwise at distance>r. Uniform almost-wideness replaces those flips by deletion of ≤s vertices. Structurally means contained in an FO-transduction of such a class: finitely many unary colors, then fixed FO vertex/edge formulas without copying.

**Source.** Fatemeh Ghasemi; Julien Grange; Mamadou Moustapha Kanté; Florent Madelaine. *Weakly-Sparse and Strongly Flip-Flat Classes of Graphs Are Uniformly Almost-Wide*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.41) Location: Conjecture 2, p. 41:3; Eleftheriadis thesis Question 6, p. 171.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2380, checked 6 October 2026: retained in inspected version; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04610v1) · [2](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/b025bf2b-fb9e-4f68-84a3-172334d26023)

## Q2381. Universal-cover component as truncation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 27) · **Collection** 24

For every n≥1 and pointed connected type A, is the connected component of p̃ in Covering_n(A) equivalent to ∥A∥\_{n+1}?

**Context.** Origin for question 2381: Conjecture 27; n=0 established in Remark 29. Setup for question 2381: Work in univalent homotopy type theory with truncations. For n≥0 and pointed connected A, Covering_n(A) is the type of maps p:B→A with n-truncated fibers, without chosen points. The universal n-cover p̃ has domain Σ\_{x:A}∥⋆=x∥\_n. Its connected component is Σ\_{p:Covering_n(A)}∥p=p̃∥\_{−1}.

**Source.** Samuel Mimram; Émile Oleon. *Classifying Covering Types in Homotopy Type Theory*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10064v2) Location: Conjecture 27, p. 9 of arXiv v2; Remark 29 covers n=0.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2381, checked 6 October 2026: retained in inspected version; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.21)

## Q2382. Finitely bounded CSP representatives

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Is every extensional ESO problem polynomial-time many-one equivalent to CSP(B) for some finitely bounded relational structure B?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2382, 2383: Preprint 2025 §8; LICS 2026 §8, questions 2 and 5. Setup for questions 2382, 2383: An extensional ESO problem fixes an FO sentence θ and designated relations: on finite input A, ask whether a same-domain B satisfies θ, extends designated A-relations, and preserves all others. B is finitely bounded if finitely many forbidden induced substructures characterize its finite substructures. CSP(B) is finite-input homomorphism existence. SNP has universal FO part; monotone means input predicates occur negatively; connected means each clause’s negative-literal database is connected after equality identifications.

**Source.** Manuel Bodirsky; Santiago Guzmán-Pro. *On the Computational Power of Extensional ESO*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.LICS.2026.20) Location: §8(2), p. 20:24.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2382, 2383, checked 6 October 2026: July proceedings retain both; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08515)

## Q2469. Strength of inhomogeneous ring regularity

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

What is the reverse-mathematical strength over RCA₀ of the assertion: for every commutative ring R, finite m×n matrix A over R and nonzero b∈R^m, if Ax=b has no constant solution, some finite coloring of R has no monochromatic solution?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2469: Gabriela Laboska. PhD, University of Chicago, 2025. Advisors: Maryanthe Malliaris; Denis Hirschfeldt. Origin for question 2469: Leader–Russell 2020; effective questions Laboska 2025. Setup for question 2469: A monochromatic solution has all coordinates the same color; a constant solution has all coordinates equal. Rings and algebraic operations are countable coded structures.

**Source.** Gabriela Laboska. *On the Effectiveness of Partition Regularity over Algebraic Structures*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.10178) Location: Open Question 3, p. 5; §2.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2469, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: WKL0 proves the retained ring-coloring assertion over RCA0, with at most [3(n-1)]^(n-1) colors for n>=2 and one color for n<=1. A finite-presentation construction avoids comprehension of the range sR; supplied finite integer matrices are diagonalized by an explicit primitive-recursive algorithm. Every PA degree relative to the input can compute a witnessing coloring. Restricted scope: Countable coded commutative rings, as required for the retained second-order arithmetic statement; no additional ring hypothesis. Only an upper bound on logical strength is proved. Remaining: No WKL0 reversal, lower bound on the optimal color count, or exact reverse-mathematical classification is proved. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R5: auxiliary proof submission): Over RCA0, WKL0 implies the ring-coloring assertion, with q(n)=[3(n-1)]^(n-1) colors for n>=2. The finite feasibility lemma is proved by explicit integer-matrix diagonalization. Location: Part E. Restricted scope: Only the upper logical bound is proved. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://knowledge.uchicago.edu/records/7rfhc-ymn78)

## Q2470. Maximal complexity of hyperfiniteness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.2.3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Is the set of Borel codes for hyperfinite CBERs on Baire space Σ¹₂-complete?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2470, 2471: Cecelia Higgins. PhD, University of California, Los Angeles, 2025. Advisors: Andrew Marks; Anton Bernshteyn. Origin for questions 2470, 2471: Dougherty–Jackson–Kechris 1994; Weiss 1984. Setup for questions 2470, 2471: A CBER is a countable Borel equivalence relation on a standard Borel space. It is hyperfinite if it is an increasing countable union of Borel equivalence relations with finite classes.

**Source.** Cecelia Higgins. *Measurable Brooks’s Theorem for Directed Graphs and the Complexity of Finite Borel Asymptotic Dimension*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2025. Advisor(s): Andrew Marks; Anton Bernshteyn. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/7w93q45v) Location: Question 3.2.3, p. 54.

## Q2472. Bounded-degree dimension complexity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Is the set of codes for bounded-degree Borel graphs with finite Borel asymptotic dimension Σ¹₂-complete?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2472: see question 2470. Origin for question 2472: Preprint 2024; Question 6.1. Setup for question 2472: For a Borel graph G, asdim_B(G)≤d means: for every integer r≥1 there is a Borel partition into d+1 sets whose components in the distance≤r graph have uniformly bounded G-diameter.
